# Colombia

This article is about the country. For other uses, see [Colombia (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombia_\(disambiguation\)).

Not to be confused with [Columbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_\(disambiguation\)) or [Colombo](56636.md).

**Colombia**,[^cite_note-17] officially the **Republic of Colombia**,[^cite_note-18] is a country located in [South America](26769.md), with [insular regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insular_region_of_Colombia) in [North America](21139.md). Colombia's mainland is bordered by the [Caribbean Sea](7822.md) to the north, [Venezuela](32374.md) to the east, [Brazil](3383.md) to the southeast, [Peru](170691.md) and [Ecuador](9334.md) to the south and southwest, the [Pacific Ocean](23070.md) to the west, and [Panama](22997.md) to the northwest. Colombia is divided into 32 [departments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Departments_of_Colombia). The Capital District of [Bogotá](211271.md) is the [country's largest city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Colombia_by_population) hosting the main financial and cultural hub. Other urban areas include [Medellín](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medell%C3%ADn), [Cali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cali), [Barranquilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barranquilla), [Cartagena](63927.md), [Bucaramanga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bucaramanga), [Pereira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pereira,_Colombia), [Santa Marta](547214.md), [Cúcuta](623811.md), [Ibagué](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibagu%C3%A9), [Villavicencio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villavicencio) and [Manizales](1116015.md). It covers an area of 1,141,748 square kilometers (440,831 sq mi) and has a population of around 52 million. Its rich [cultural heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_heritage)[^cite_note-19]—including language, religion, cuisine, and art—reflects its history as a colony, fusing cultural elements brought by [immigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_to_Colombia) from Europe[^cite_note-20][^cite_note-21][^cite_note-22][^cite_note-23] and the [Middle East](19323.md),[^cite_note-Posada2-24][^cite_note-25][^cite_note-26] with those brought by the [African diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_diaspora),[^cite_note-27] as well as with those of [Indigenous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas) civilizations that predate colonization.[^cite_note-28] [Spanish](26825.md) is the [official language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_language), although [Creole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Andr%C3%A9s%E2%80%93Providencia_Creole), [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language) and [64 other languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Colombia) are recognized regionally.

Colombia has been home to many [Indigenous peoples and cultures](4053513.md) since at least 12,000 BCE. The Spanish landed in [La Guajira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guajira_Peninsula) in 1499, and by the mid-16th century had colonized much of present-day Colombia, establishing the [New Kingdom of Granada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Kingdom_of_Granada). [Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_War_of_Independence) from the [Spanish Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Empire) was declared in 1810, with what is now Colombia emerging as the [United Provinces of New Granada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Provinces_of_New_Granada). After a [Spanish reconquest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_reconquest_of_New_Granada), Colombian independence was secured and the [Gran Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gran_Colombia) period began in 1819. The new polity experimented with federalism as the [Granadine Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Granadine_Confederation) (1858) and then the [United States of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_of_Colombia) (1863), before becoming a [centralised republic in 1886](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Constitution_of_1886). With the backing of the US and France, [Panama seceded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secession_of_Panama_from_Colombia) from Colombia in 1903, resulting in Colombia's present borders. Beginning in the 1960s, the country has suffered from an asymmetric low-intensity [armed conflict](1857628.md) and political violence, both of which escalated in the 1990s. Since 2000, there has been improvement in security, stability, and rule of law, as well as economic growth and development.[^cite_note-Enough_Already!-29][^cite_note-Colombia's_GDP_growth-30]

Colombia is recognized for its [healthcare system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_care_in_Colombia), being the best healthcare in [the Americas](29833.md) according to the [World Health Organization](33583.md) and 22nd in the world.[^cite_note-31][^cite_note-32] Its [diversified economy](5839.md) is the third-largest in South America, with macroeconomic stability and favorable long-term growth prospects.[^cite_note-GDP-33][^cite_note-strongmacroeconomicmanagement-34] The country has one of the widest recognitions of [LGBT rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_rights_by_country_or_territory), [gender equality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_equality), [animal rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animal_rights), and [human rights](13831.md), permitting [same-sex adoption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_adoption), [same-sex marriage](92656.md), [legality of abortion](697618.md), [legality of euthanasia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legality_of_euthanasia), and [animal rights protections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animal_rights_movement).[^cite_note-35][^cite_note-36][^cite_note-37][^cite_note-38][^cite_note-39][^cite_note-40]

Colombia is one of the world's seventeen [megadiverse countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megadiverse_countries); it has the highest level of [biodiversity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biodiversity_of_Colombia) per square mile in the world, and second-highest level overall.[^cite_note-Biodiversity_of_Colombia-41] Its territory encompasses the [Amazon rainforest](9299263.md), [highlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andean_natural_region), [grasslands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orinoqu%C3%ADa_natural_region) and [deserts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatacoa_Desert). Colombia is a member of global and regional organizations including the [UN](31769.md), the [WTO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization), the [OECD](33853117.md), the [OAS](22788.md), the [CIVETS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIVETS), the [Pacific Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Alliance) and the [Andean Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andean_Community); it is a [NATO](21133.md) Global Partner[^cite_note-42] and a [major non-NATO ally](3812445.md) of the [United States](3434750.md).[^cite_note-\:1-43]

## Etymology

The name "Colombia" is derived from the last name of the Italian navigator [Christopher Columbus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Columbus) ([Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_language): *Christophorus Columbus*, [Italian](14708.md): *Cristoforo Colombo*, Spanish: *Cristóbal Colón*). It was conceived as a reference to all of the New World.[^cite_note-44] The name was later adopted by the [Republic of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gran_Colombia) of 1819, formed from the territories of the old [Viceroyalty of New Granada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viceroyalty_of_New_Granada) (modern-day Colombia, Panama, Venezuela, Ecuador, and northwest Brazil).[^cite_note-LABLAA5-45]

When Venezuela, Ecuador, and [Cundinamarca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cundinamarca_Department_\(1820\)) came to exist as independent states, the former [Department of Cundinamarca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_of_the_Republic_of_New_Granada) adopted the name "[Republic of New Granada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_New_Granada)". New Granada officially changed its name in 1858 to the [Granadine Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Granadine_Confederation). In 1863 the name was again changed, this time to [United States of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_of_Colombia), before finally adopting its present name – the Republic of Colombia – in 1886.[^cite_note-LABLAA5-45]

To refer to this country, the Colombian government uses the terms *Colombia* and *República de Colombia*.[^cite_note-46]

## History

Main articles: [History of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Colombia) and [Timeline of Colombian history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Colombian_history)

### Pre-Columbian era

Main article: [Pre-Columbian cultures of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pre-Columbian_cultures_of_Colombia)



Location map of the pre-Columbian cultures of Colombia

Owing to its location, the present territory of Colombia was a corridor of early human civilization from [Mesoamerica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesoamerica) and the [Caribbean](18956035.md) to the [Andes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andes) and [Amazon basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_basin). The oldest archaeological finds are from the Pubenza and El Totumo sites in the Magdalena Valley 100 kilometers (62 mi) southwest of Bogotá.[^cite_note-47] These sites date from the [Paleoindian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleo-Indians) period (18,000–8000 BCE). At [Puerto Hormiga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerto_Hormiga_archaeological_site) and other sites, traces from the [Archaic Period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaic_Period_\(Americas\)) (\~8000–2000 BCE) have been found. Vestiges indicate that there was also early occupation in the regions of [El Abra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Abra) and [Tequendama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tequendama) in [Cundinamarca](189405.md). The oldest pottery discovered in the Americas, found in [San Jacinto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Jacinto,_Bol%C3%ADvar), dates to 5000–4000 BCE.[^cite_note-48][^cite_note-49]

Indigenous people inhabited the territory that is now Colombia by 12,500 BCE. Nomadic [hunter-gatherer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter-gatherer) tribes at the El Abra, [Tibitó](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tibit%C3%B3) and Tequendama sites near present-day [Bogotá](211271.md) traded with one another and with other cultures from the [Magdalena River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magdalena_River) Valley.[^cite_note-50] A site including eight miles (13 km) of [pictographs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pictogram) that is under study at Serranía de la Lindosa was revealed in November 2020.[^cite_note-51] Their age is suggested as being 12,500 years old (c. 10,480 B.C.) by the anthropologists working on the site, because of extinct fauna depicted. It was during the earliest known human occupation of the area.

Between 5000 and 1000 BCE, hunter-gatherer tribes transitioned to agrarian societies; fixed settlements were established, and pottery appeared. Beginning in the 1st millennium BCE, groups of [Amerindians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas) including the [Muisca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muisca), [Zenú](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zen%C3%BA), [Quimbaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quimbaya_civilization), and [Tairona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tairona) developed the political system of *[cacicazgos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cacicazgo)* with a pyramidal structure of power headed by *caciques*. The Muisca inhabited mainly the area of what is now the [Departments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Departments_of_Colombia) of [Boyacá](185665.md) and [Cundinamarca](189405.md) high plateau (*[Altiplano Cundiboyacense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Altiplano_Cundiboyacense)*) where they formed the [Muisca Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muisca_Confederation). They farmed maize, potato, quinoa, and cotton, and traded gold, [emeralds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_emeralds), blankets, ceramic handicrafts, coca and especially [rock salt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halite) with neighboring nations. The Tairona inhabited northern Colombia in the isolated mountain range of [Sierra Nevada de Santa Marta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sierra_Nevada_de_Santa_Marta).[^cite_note-52][^cite_note-53] The Quimbaya inhabited regions of the [Cauca River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cauca_River) Valley between the [Western](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cordillera_Occidental_\(Colombia\)) and [Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cordillera_Central_\(Colombia\)) Ranges of the Colombian Andes.[^cite_note-54] Most of the Amerindians practiced agriculture and the social structure of each Indigenous community was different. Some groups of Indigenous people such as the Caribs lived in a state of permanent war, but others had less bellicose attitudes.[^cite_note-55] During the 1200s, [Malayo-Polynesians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austronesian_expansion) and [Amerindians](4053513.md) in Colombia made contact, thereby spreading Indigeous Amerindian genetics from Precolonial Colombia to some Pacific Ocean islands.[^cite_note-56][^cite_note-57] The coconuts found in Colombia which originally come from the [Philippines](23440.md) originate from this Precolombian exchange.[^cite_note-58]

### Colonial period

Main articles: [New Kingdom of Granada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Kingdom_of_Granada) and [Viceroyalty of New Granada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viceroyalty_of_New_Granada)

See also: [Spanish conquest of New Granada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_conquest_of_New_Granada), [Spanish conquest of the Muisca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_conquest_of_the_Muisca), [Spanish colonization of the Americas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_colonization_of_the_Americas), and [Spanish Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Empire)



[Vasco Núñez de Balboa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasco_N%C3%BA%C3%B1ez_de_Balboa), founder of [Santa María la Antigua del Darién](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Mar%C3%ADa_la_Antigua_del_Dari%C3%A9n), the first stable European settlement on the continent

[Alonso de Ojeda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alonso_de_Ojeda) (who had sailed with Columbus) reached the [Guajira Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guajira_Peninsula) in 1499.[^cite_note-LABLAA6-59][^cite_note-60] Spanish explorers, led by [Rodrigo de Bastidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodrigo_de_Bastidas), made the first exploration of the [Caribbean coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caribbean_Basin) in 1500.[^cite_note-61] [Christopher Columbus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Columbus) navigated near the Caribbean in 1502.[^cite_note-62] In 1508, [Vasco Núñez de Balboa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasco_N%C3%BA%C3%B1ez_de_Balboa) accompanied an expedition to the territory through the region of [Gulf of Urabá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Urab%C3%A1) and they founded the town of [Santa María la Antigua del Darién](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Mar%C3%ADa_la_Antigua_del_Dari%C3%A9n) in 1510, the first stable settlement on the continent.[^cite_note-63][^cite_note-64] [Santa Marta](547214.md) was founded in 1525,[^cite_note-65] and [Cartagena](63927.md) in 1533.[^cite_note-66] Spanish [conquistador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conquistador) [Gonzalo Jiménez de Quesada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gonzalo_Jim%C3%A9nez_de_Quesada) led an expedition to the interior in April 1536, and christened the districts through which he passed "[New Kingdom of Granada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Kingdom_of_Granada)". In August 1538, he provisionally founded its capital near the Muisca [cacicazgo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cacicazgo) of [Muyquytá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bacat%C3%A1), and named it "Santa Fe". The name soon acquired a suffix and was called Santa Fe de Bogotá.[^cite_note-67][^cite_note-JaramilloUribe1989-68] Two other notable journeys by early conquistadors to the interior took place in the same period. [Sebastián de Belalcázar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebasti%C3%A1n_de_Belalc%C3%A1zar), conqueror of [Quito](209982.md), traveled north and founded [Cali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cali), in 1536, and [Popayán](657155.md), in 1537;[^cite_note-69] from 1536 to 1539, German conquistador [Nikolaus Federmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikolaus_Federmann) crossed the [Llanos Orientales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orinoqu%C3%ADa_natural_region) and went over the [Cordillera Oriental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cordillera_Oriental_\(Colombia\)) in a search for [El Dorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Dorado), the "city of gold".[^cite_note-70][^cite_note-71] The legend and the gold would play a pivotal role in luring the Spanish and other Europeans to New Granada during the 16th and 17th centuries.[^cite_note-72]

The [conquistadors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_conquistadors_in_Colombia) made frequent alliances with the enemies of different indigenous communities. [Indigenous allies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_auxiliaries) were crucial to conquest, as well as to creating and maintaining empire.[^cite_note-73] Indigenous peoples in Colombia experienced a decline in population due to conquest as well as Eurasian diseases, such as [smallpox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smallpox), to which they had no immunity.[^cite_note-74][^cite_note-75] Regarding the land as deserted, the Spanish Crown sold properties to all persons interested in colonized territories, creating large farms and possession of mines.[^cite_note-TierrasColonia-76][^cite_note-EconomíaColonial-77][^cite_note-políticaeconómica-78] In the 16th century, the [nautical science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navigation) in Spain reached a great development thanks to numerous scientific figures of the [Casa de Contratación](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casa_de_Contrataci%C3%B3n) and nautical science was an essential pillar of the [Iberian expansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iberian_ship_development,_1400%E2%80%931600).[^cite_note-Alonso_de_Santa_Cruz-79] In 1542, the region of New Granada, along with all other Spanish possessions in South America, became part of the [Viceroyalty of Peru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viceroyalty_of_Peru), with its capital in [Lima](85423.md).[^cite_note-80] In 1547, New Granada became a separate captaincy-general within the viceroyalty, with its capital at Santa Fe de Bogota.[^cite_note-81] In 1549, the [Royal Audiencia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Audiencia) was created by a royal decree, and New Granada was ruled by the [Royal Audience of Santa Fe de Bogotá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Audience_of_Santa_Fe_de_Bogot%C3%A1), which at that time comprised the provinces of Santa Marta, Rio de San Juan, Popayán, Guayana and Cartagena.[^cite_note-82] But important decisions were taken from the colony to Spain by the [Council of the Indies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_the_Indies).[^cite_note-83][^cite_note-84]



An illustration of the [Battle of Cartagena de Indias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Cartagena_de_Indias), a major Spanish victory in the [War of Jenkins' Ear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_of_Jenkins'_Ear)

[^cite_note-BattleofCartagena-85]

In the 16th century, European slave traders had begun to bring [enslaved Africans](49005.md) to the Americas. Spain was the only European power that did not establish [factories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Factory_\(trading_post\)) in Africa to purchase slaves; the Spanish Empire instead relied on the [asiento](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asiento) system, awarding merchants from other European nations the license to trade enslaved peoples to their overseas territories.[^cite_note-86][^cite_note-87] This system brought Africans to Colombia, although many spoke out against the institution.[^cite_note-90][^cite_note-93] The indigenous peoples could not be enslaved because they were legally [subjects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commoners) of the Spanish Crown.[^cite_note-94] To protect the indigenous peoples, several forms of land ownership and regulation were established by the Spanish colonial authorities: *resguardos*, *encomiendas* and *haciendas*.[^cite_note-TierrasColonia-76][^cite_note-EconomíaColonial-77][^cite_note-políticaeconómica-78]

However, secret anti-Spanish discontentment was already brewing for Colombians since Spain prohibited direct trade between the [Viceroyalty of Peru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viceroyalty_of_Peru), which included Colombia, and the [Viceroyalty of New Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viceroyalty_of_New_Spain), which included the Philippines, the source of Asian products like silk and porcelain which was in demand in the Americas. Illegal trade between Peruvians, Filipinos, and Mexicans continued in secret, as smuggled Asian goods ended up in [Córdoba, Colombia](549670.md), the distribution center for illegal Asian imports, due to the collusion between these peoples against the authorities in Spain. They settled and traded with each other while disobeying the forced Spanish monopoly.[^cite_note-95]



Map of the [Viceroyalty of New Granada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viceroyalty_of_New_Granada)

The [Viceroyalty of New Granada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viceroyalty_of_New_Granada) was established in 1717, then temporarily removed, and then re-established in 1739. Its capital was Santa Fé de Bogotá. This Viceroyalty included some other provinces of northwestern South America that had previously been under the jurisdiction of the [Viceroyalties of New Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Spain) or [Peru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viceroyalty_of_Peru) and correspond mainly to today's Venezuela, Ecuador, and Panama. Bogotá became one of the principal administrative centers of the Spanish possessions in the New World, along with [Lima](85423.md) and [Mexico City](18987.md), though it remained less developed compared to those two cities in several economic and logistical ways.[^cite_note-96][^cite_note-97]

[Great Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Great_Britain) declared war [on Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_of_Jenkins'_Ear) in 1739, and the city of Cartagena quickly became a top target for the British. A massive British expeditionary force was dispatched to capture the city, but, after achieving initial inroads, devastating outbreaks of disease crippled their numbers, and the British were forced to withdraw. The battle became one of Spain's most decisive victories in the conflict, and secured Spanish dominance in the Caribbean until the [Seven Years' War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_Years'_War).[^cite_note-BattleofCartagena-85][^cite_note-98] The 18th-century priest, botanist, and mathematician [José Celestino Mutis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Celestino_Mutis) was delegated by Viceroy [Antonio Caballero y Góngora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Caballero_y_G%C3%B3ngora) to conduct an inventory of the nature of New Granada. Started in 1783, this became known as the [Royal Botanical Expedition to New Granada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Botanical_Expedition_to_New_Granada). It classified plants and wildlife, and founded the first astronomical observatory in the city of Santa Fe de Bogotá.[^cite_note-99] In July 1801 the Prussian scientist [Alexander von Humboldt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_von_Humboldt) reached Santa Fe de Bogotá where he met with Mutis. In addition, historical figures in the process of independence in New Granada emerged from the expedition as the astronomer [Francisco José de Caldas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Jos%C3%A9_de_Caldas), the scientist [Francisco Antonio Zea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Antonio_Zea), the zoologist [Jorge Tadeo Lozano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorge_Tadeo_Lozano) and the painter [Salvador Rizo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salvador_Rizo).[^cite_note-100][^cite_note-101]

### Independence

Main articles: [Colombian War of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_War_of_Independence), [First Republic of New Granada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Republic_of_New_Granada), and [Gran Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gran_Colombia)

|  | **The [accessibility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Manual_of_Style/Accessibility) of this section is in question**. Relevant discussion may be found on the [talk page](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk\:Colombia#). *(November 2024)* |
| --- | --- |



The departments of Gran Colombia in 1824

Rebellions against Spanish rule had occurred in the empire since the advent of conquest and colonization, but most were either crushed or remained too weak to change the overall situation. The last one that sought outright independence from Spain sprang up around 1810 and culminated in the Colombian Declaration of Independence, issued on 20 July 1810, the day that is now celebrated as the nation's Independence Day.[^cite_note-102] This movement followed the independence of [Saint-Domingue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint-Domingue) (present-day Haiti) in 1804, which provided some support to an eventual leader of this rebellion: [Simón Bolívar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sim%C3%B3n_Bol%C3%ADvar). [Francisco de Paula Santander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_de_Paula_Santander) also would play a decisive role.[^cite_note-103][^cite_note-104][^cite_note-105]

A movement was initiated by [Antonio Nariño](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Nari%C3%B1o), who opposed Spanish centralism and led the opposition against the [Viceroyalty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viceroyalty).[^cite_note-106] [Cartagena](63927.md) became independent in November 1811.[^cite_note-107] In 1811, the [United Provinces of New Granada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Provinces_of_New_Granada) were proclaimed, headed by [Camilo Torres Tenorio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camilo_Torres_Tenorio).[^cite_note-108][^cite_note-109] The emergence of two distinct ideological currents among the patriots ([federalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federalism) and [centralism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_state)) gave rise to a period of instability called the [Patria Boba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patria_Boba).[^cite_note-110] Shortly after the [Napoleonic Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleonic_Wars) ended, [Ferdinand VII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferdinand_VII), recently restored to the throne in Spain, unexpectedly decided [to send military forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_reconquest_of_New_Granada) to retake most of northern South America. The viceroyalty was restored under the command of [Juan de Sámano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_de_S%C3%A1mano), whose regime punished those who participated in the patriotic movements, ignoring the political nuances of the [juntas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junta_\(Spanish_American_Independence\)).[^cite_note-111] The retribution stoked renewed rebellion, which, combined with a weakened Spain, made possible a successful rebellion led by the Venezuelan-born [Simón Bolívar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_career_of_Sim%C3%B3n_Bol%C3%ADvar), who finally proclaimed [independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_American_wars_of_independence) in 1819.[^cite_note-Historia_ilustrada_de_Colombia-112][^cite_note-113] The [pro-Spanish resistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royalist_\(Spanish_American_Independence\)) was defeated in 1822 in the present territory of Colombia and in 1823 in Venezuela.[^cite_note-114][^cite_note-115][^cite_note-116] During the Independence War, between 250 and 400 thousand people died (12–20% of the pre-war population).[^cite_note-19thcentury-117][^cite_note-Deremilitari-118][^cite_note-119]



Changes to the borders of Colombia between 1811 and 2012

The territory of the Viceroyalty of New Granada became the [Republic of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gran_Colombia), organized as a [union of the current territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subdivisions_of_Gran_Colombia) of Colombia, Panama, Ecuador, Venezuela, parts of Guyana and Brazil and north of [Marañón River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mara%C3%B1%C3%B3n_River).[^cite_note-120] The [Congress of Cúcuta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congress_of_C%C3%BAcuta) in 1821 adopted a [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Constitution_of_1821) for the new Republic.[^cite_note-121][^cite_note-122] Simón Bolívar became the first [President of Colombia](251302.md), and Francisco de Paula Santander was made [Vice President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_President_of_Colombia).[^cite_note-123] However, the new republic was unstable and the Gran Colombia ultimately collapsed.

Modern Colombia comes from one of the countries that emerged after the [dissolution of Gran Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_Gran_Colombia), the other two being Ecuador and Venezuela.[^cite_note-EtHisColombia-124][^cite_note-GranColombiaNuevaGranada-125][^cite_note-126] Colombia was the first [constitutional government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_government) in South America,[^cite_note-HistoriaConstitucional-127] and the [Liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Liberal_Party) and [Conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Conservative_Party) parties, founded in 1848 and 1849, respectively, are two of the oldest surviving political parties in the Americas.[^cite_note-128] [Slavery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery) was abolished in the country in 1851.[^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130]

Internal political and territorial divisions led to the dissolution of [Gran Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gran_Colombia) in 1830.[^cite_note-EtHisColombia-124][^cite_note-GranColombiaNuevaGranada-125] The so-called "[Department of Cundinamarca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cundinamarca_Province)" adopted the name "[New Granada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_the_New_Granada)", which it kept until 1858 when it became the "Confederación Granadina" ([Granadine Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Granadine_Confederation)). After a [two-year civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Civil_War_\(1860%E2%80%931862\)) in 1863, the [United States of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_of_Colombia) was created, which became known as the Republic of Colombia in 1886.[^cite_note-HistoriaConstitucional-127][^cite_note-131] Internal divisions remained between the bipartisan political forces, occasionally igniting very bloody civil wars, the most significant being the [Thousand Days' War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thousand_Days'_War) (1899–1902), in which between 100 and 180 thousand Colombians lost their lives when the [Liberal Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Liberal_Party), supported by [Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_of_Venezuela), [Ecuador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Ecuador_\(1895%E2%80%931925\)), [Nicaragua](21362.md), and [Guatemala](17238567.md) rebelled against the [Nationalist government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Conservative_Party) and took control of [Santander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santander_State), ultimately being defeated in 1902 by nationalist forces.[^cite_note-132]

### 20th century

See also: [Colombian conflict](1857628.md) and [La Violencia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Violencia)

The United States' efforts to assert influence over the region, particularly regarding the construction and control of the [Panama Canal](24849.md),[^cite_note-133] contributed to the [secession of the Department of Panama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secession_of_Panama_from_Colombia) in 1903 and its political independence.[^cite_note-SeparationofPanama-134] The United States paid Colombia \$25,000,000 in 1921, seven years after completion of the canal, as redress for President [Theodore Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodore_Roosevelt)'s role in the creation of Panama, and Colombia recognized Panama under the terms of the [Thomson–Urrutia Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomson%E2%80%93Urrutia_Treaty).[^cite_note-135] Colombia and Peru went to [war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leticia_Incident) because of territory disputes far in the [Amazon basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_basin). The war ended with a peace deal brokered by the [League of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_Nations). The League finally awarded the disputed area to Colombia in June 1934.[^cite_note-136]



The [Bogotazo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bogotazo) in 1948

Soon after, Colombia achieved some degree of political stability, which was interrupted by a bloody conflict that took place between the late 1940s and the early 1950s, a period known as *[La Violencia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Violencia)* ("The Violence"). Its cause was mainly mounting tensions between the two leading political parties, which subsequently ignited after the assassination of the Liberal presidential candidate [Jorge Eliécer Gaitán](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorge_Eli%C3%A9cer_Gait%C3%A1n) on 9 April 1948.[^cite_note-137][^cite_note-138] The ensuing riots in Bogotá, known as [El Bogotazo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Bogotazo), spread throughout the country and claimed the lives of at least 180,000 Colombians.[^cite_note-Encarta-139]

Colombia entered the [Korean War](16772.md) when [Laureano Gómez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laureano_G%C3%B3mez) was elected president. It was the only Latin American country to join the war in a direct military role as an ally of the United States. Particularly important was the resistance of the Colombian troops at [Old Baldy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Old_Baldy).[^cite_note-Colombia's_legacy_with_Korea-140]

The violence between the two political parties decreased first when [Gustavo Rojas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gustavo_Rojas_Pinilla) deposed the [President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laureano_G%C3%B3mez) of Colombia in a coup d'état and negotiated with the guerrillas, and then under the [military junta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Military_Junta) of General [Gabriel París](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_Par%C3%ADs).[^cite_note-141][^cite_note-142]



The Axis of Peace and Memory, a memorial to the victims of the [Colombian conflict](1857628.md) (1964–present)

After Rojas' deposition, the Colombian Conservative Party and the Colombian Liberal Party agreed to create the [National Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Front_\(Colombia\)), a coalition that would jointly govern the country. Under the deal, the presidency would alternate between conservatives and liberals every 4 years for 16 years; the two parties would have parity in all other elective offices.[^cite_note-143] The National Front ended "La Violencia", and National Front administrations attempted to institute far-reaching social and economic reforms in cooperation with the [Alliance for Progress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_for_Progress).[^cite_note-144][^cite_note-145] Despite the progress in certain sectors, many social and political problems continued, and guerrilla groups were formally created such as the [FARC](26100.md), the [ELN](822032.md) and the [M-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/19th_of_April_Movement) to fight the government and political apparatus.[^cite_note-146]

Since the 1960s, the country has suffered from an [asymmetric](46863.md) [low-intensity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Low_intensity_conflict) [armed conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_armed_conflict_\(1964%E2%80%93present\)) between [government forces](5843.md), [leftist guerrilla groups](13266029.md) and [right wing paramilitaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramilitarism_in_Colombia).[^cite_note-HistoricalCommission-147] The conflict escalated in the 1990s,[^cite_note-Colombian_armed_conflict-148] mainly in remote rural areas.[^cite_note-Colombian_conflict-149] Since the beginning of the armed conflict, [human rights defenders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_defenders) have fought for the respect for human rights, despite staggering opposition.[^cite_note-152][^cite_note-154] Several guerrillas' organizations decided to demobilize after peace negotiations in 1989–1994.[^cite_note-Enough_Already!-29]

The United States has been heavily involved in the conflict since its beginnings, when in the early 1960s the [U.S. government](195149.md) encouraged the Colombian military to attack leftist militias in rural Colombia. This was part of the U.S. fight against communism. [Mercenaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercenarie) and multinational corporations such as [Chiquita Brands International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiquita_Brands_International) are some of the international actors that have contributed to the violence of the conflict.[^cite_note-HistoricalCommission-147][^cite_note-Enough_Already!-29][^cite_note-colombia-and-us-54-155]

Beginning in the mid-1970s Colombian [drug cartels](197015.md) became major producers, processors and exporters of [illegal drugs](13005346.md), primarily [marijuana](1481886.md) and [cocaine](7701.md).[^cite_note-156]

On 4 July 1991, a new [Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Constitution_of_1991) was promulgated. The changes generated by the new constitution are viewed as positive by Colombian society.[^cite_note-157][^cite_note-Colombian_Constitution_of_1991-158]

### 21st century

See also: [Colombian peace process](51422824.md)



Former President [Juan Manuel Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Manuel_Santos) signing a [peace accord](51422824.md)

The administration of President [Álvaro Uribe](243680.md) (2002–2010) adopted the [democratic security policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_security_policy) which included an integrated [counter-terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counter-terrorism) and [counter-insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counter-insurgency) campaign.[^cite_note-159] The government economic plan also promoted confidence in investors.[^cite_note-160] As part of a controversial peace process, the [AUC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Self-Defense_Forces_of_Colombia) (right-wing paramilitaries) had ceased to function formally as an organization .[^cite_note-CIAWFB-161] In February 2008, millions of Colombians demonstrated against FARC and other outlawed groups.[^cite_note-162]

After peace negotiations in [Cuba](5042481.md), the [Colombian government](76741.md) of President [Juan Manuel Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Manuel_Santos) and the [guerrillas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guerrillas) of the FARC-EP announced a final agreement to end the conflict.[^cite_note-163] However, a [referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_peace_agreement_referendum,_2016) to ratify the deal was unsuccessful.[^cite_note-164][^cite_note-165] Afterward, the Colombian government and the FARC signed a revised [peace deal](51422824.md) in November 2016,[^cite_note-166] which the [Colombian congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_congress) approved.[^cite_note-167] In 2016, President Santos was awarded the [Nobel Peace Prize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Nobel_Peace_Prize).[^cite_note-168] The Government began a process of attention and comprehensive reparation for victims of conflict.[^cite_note-169][^cite_note-170] Colombia shows modest progress in the struggle to defend human rights, as expressed by [HRW](46530.md).[^cite_note-171] A [Special Jurisdiction of Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Jurisdiction_of_Peace) has been created to investigate, clarify, prosecute and punish serious human rights violations and grave breaches of [international humanitarian law](1093604.md) which occurred during the armed conflict and to satisfy victims' right to [justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justice).[^cite_note-172] During his visit to Colombia, [Pope Francis](1687680.md) paid tribute to the victims of the conflict.[^cite_note-173]



[Gustavo Petro](13391962.md), the country's first [left-wing](18499.md) president

In June 2018, [Iván Duque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iv%C3%A1n_Duque), the candidate of the right-wing [Democratic Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Center_\(Colombia\)) party, won the presidential [election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Colombian_presidential_election).[^cite_note-174] On 7 August 2018, he was sworn in as the new [President of Colombia](251302.md) to succeed Juan Manuel Santos.[^cite_note-175] [Colombia's relations with Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Colombia%E2%80%93Venezuela_diplomatic_crisis) have fluctuated due to ideological differences between the two governments.[^cite_note-176] Colombia has offered [humanitarian support](434679.md) with food and medicines to mitigate the shortage of supplies in Venezuela.[^cite_note-177] Colombia's Foreign Ministry said that all efforts to resolve [Venezuela's crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crisis_in_Venezuela_\(2012%E2%80%93present\)) should be peaceful.[^cite_note-178] Colombia proposed the idea of the [Sustainable Development Goals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sustainable_Development_Goals) and a final document was adopted by the United Nations.[^cite_note-179] In February 2019, Venezuelan president [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) cut off diplomatic relations with Colombia after Colombian President Ivan Duque had helped Venezuelan opposition politicians deliver humanitarian aid to their country. Colombia recognized Venezuelan opposition leader [Juan Guaidó](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Guaid%C3%B3) as the country's legitimate president. In January 2020, Colombia rejected Maduro's proposal that the two countries restore diplomatic relations.[^cite_note-180]

[Protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Colombian_protests) started on 28 April 2021 when the government proposed a tax bill that would greatly expand the range of the 19 percent [value-added tax](52177473.md).[^cite_note-nyb220721-181] The [2022 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Colombian_presidential_election) run-off vote ended in a win for former guerrilla [Gustavo Petro](13391962.md), who received 50.47% of the vote compared to 47.27% for independent candidate [Rodolfo Hernández](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodolfo_Hern%C3%A1ndez_Su%C3%A1rez). The single-term limit for the country's presidency prevented President Iván Duque from seeking re-election. On 7 August 2022, Petro was sworn in, becoming the country's first leftist president.[^cite_note-182][^cite_note-183] Later that month, Colombia and Venezuela formally reestablished bilateral diplomatic relations.[^cite_note-184] The 21 June 2026 [election](79603998.md) run-off vote ended in a win for right-wing candidate, [Abelardo de la Espriella](81665680.md), taking 49.7% of the vote compared to 48.7% of left-wing [Iván Cepeda](66799376.md).[^cite_note-185] On 7 August 2026, Abelardo de la Espriella was sworn in as Colombia’s president.[^cite_note-186]

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Colombia)

See also: [Geology of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geology_of_Colombia)



[Topographic map](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Topographic_map) of Colombia

The geography of Colombia is characterized by its six main [natural regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_regions_of_Colombia) that present their unique characteristics, from the [Andes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andes) mountain range region; the [Pacific Coastal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Coast) region; the Caribbean coastal region; the *[Llanos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Llanos)* (plains); the [Amazon rainforest](48139.md) region; to the [insular area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insular_Region_\(Colombia\)), comprising islands in both the Atlantic and Pacific oceans.[^cite_note-Geography_of_Colombia-187] It shares its maritime limits with [Costa Rica](5551.md), [Nicaragua](21362.md), [Honduras](13394.md), [Jamaica](15660.md), [Haiti](13373.md), and the [Dominican Republic](8060.md).[^cite_note-Maritime_borders-188]

Colombia is bordered to the northwest by Panama, to the east by Venezuela and Brazil, and to the south by Ecuador and Peru;[^cite_note-Land_borders-189] it established its maritime boundaries with neighboring countries through seven agreements on the Caribbean Sea and three on the Pacific Ocean.[^cite_note-Maritime_borders-188] It lies between latitudes [12°N](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/12th_parallel_north) and [4°S](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4th_parallel_south) and between longitudes [67°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/67th_meridian_west) and [79°W](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/79th_meridian_west).

East of the Andes lies the [savanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Savanna) of the *[Llanos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Llanos)*, part of the [Orinoco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orinoco) River basin, and in the far southeast, the [jungle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jungle) of the [Amazon rainforest](48139.md). Together these lowlands make up over half Colombia's territory, but they contain less than 6% of the population. To the north the [Caribbean coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caribbean_Region_of_Colombia), home to 21.9% of the population and the location of the major port cities of [Barranquilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barranquilla) and [Cartagena](63927.md), generally consists of low-lying plains, but it also contains the [Sierra Nevada de Santa Marta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sierra_Nevada_de_Santa_Marta) mountain range, which includes the country's tallest peaks ([Pico Cristóbal Colón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pico_Crist%C3%B3bal_Col%C3%B3n) and [Pico Simón Bolívar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pico_Sim%C3%B3n_Bol%C3%ADvar)), and the [La Guajira Desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Guajira_Desert). By contrast the narrow and discontinuous [Pacific coastal lowlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Region_of_Colombia), backed by the [Serranía de Baudó](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baud%C3%B3_Mountains) mountains, are sparsely populated and covered in dense vegetation. The principal Pacific port is [Buenaventura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buenaventura,_Colombia).[^cite_note-Geography_of_Colombia-187][^cite_note-populationbyregions-190][^cite_note-Population_density-191]



[Sierra Nevada de Santa Marta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sierra_Nevada_de_Santa_Marta) as seen from the [ISS](15043.md)

Part of the [Ring of Fire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Ring_of_Fire), a region of the world subject to earthquakes and [volcanic eruptions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volcano),[^cite_note-Ring_of_Fire-192] in the interior of Colombia the Andes are the prevailing geographical feature. Most of Colombia's population centers are located in these interior highlands. Beyond the [Colombian Massif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Massif) (in the southwestern departments of [Cauca](549662.md) and [Nariño](549702.md)), these are divided into three branches known as *cordilleras* (mountain ranges): the [Cordillera Occidental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cordillera_Occidental_\(Colombia\)), running adjacent to the Pacific coast and including the city of [Cali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santiago_de_Cali); the [Cordillera Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cordillera_Central_\(Colombia\)), running between the [Cauca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cauca_River) and [Magdalena River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magdalena_River) valleys (to the west and east, respectively) and including the cities of [Medellín](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medell%C3%ADn), [Manizales](1116015.md), [Pereira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pereira,_Colombia), and [Armenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenia,_Quind%C3%ADo); and the [Cordillera Oriental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cordillera_Oriental_\(Colombia\)), extending northeast to the [Guajira Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guajira_Peninsula) and including Bogotá, [Bucaramanga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bucaramanga), and [Cúcuta](623811.md).[^cite_note-Geography_of_Colombia-187][^cite_note-populationbyregions-190][^cite_note-Population_density-191] Peaks in the Cordillera Occidental exceed 4,700 m (15,420 ft), and in the Cordillera Central and Cordillera Oriental they reach 5,000 m (16,404 ft). At 2,600 m (8,530 ft), Bogotá is the highest city of its size in the world.[^cite_note-Geography_of_Colombia-187]

The main rivers of Colombia are Magdalena, Cauca, [Guaviare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guaviare_River), [Atrato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atrato_River), [Meta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meta_River), [Putumayo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Putumayo_River) and [Caquetá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caquet%C3%A1_River). Colombia has four main drainage systems: the Pacific drain, the Caribbean drain, the Orinoco Basin and the Amazon Basin. The Orinoco and [Amazon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_River) Rivers mark limits with Colombia to Venezuela and Peru respectively.[^cite_note-Hydrography-193]

### Climate

Main article: [Climate of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Colombia)



Colombia map of [Köppen climate classification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification)

The climate of Colombia is characterized for being tropical presenting variations within [six natural regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_regions_of_Colombia) and depending on the altitude, temperature, [humidity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humidity), winds and rainfall.[^cite_note-Thermal_floors-194] Colombia has a diverse range of climate zones, including [tropical rainforests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_rainforest), [savannas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Savanna), [steppes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steppe), [deserts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desert) and mountain climates.

[Mountain climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_climate) is one of the unique features of the Andes and other high altitude reliefs where climate is determined by elevation. Below 1,000 meters (3,281 ft) in elevation is the [warm altitudinal zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tierra_caliente), where temperatures are above 24 °C (75.2 °F). About 82.5% of the country's total area lies in the warm altitudinal zone. The [temperate climate altitudinal zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tierra_templada) located between 1,001 and 2,000 meters (3,284 and 6,562 ft) is characterized for presenting an average temperature ranging between 17 and 24 °C (62.6 and 75.2 °F). The [cold climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tierra_fr%C3%ADa) is present between 2,001 and 3,000 meters (6,565 and 9,843 ft) and the temperatures vary between 12 and 17 °C (53.6 and 62.6 °F). Beyond lies the alpine conditions of the forested zone and then the treeless grasslands of the [páramos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P%C3%A1ramo). Above 4,000 meters (13,123 ft), where temperatures are below freezing, the climate is [glacial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tierra_helada), a zone of permanent snow and ice.[^cite_note-Thermal_floors-194]

### Biodiversity and conservation

See also: [Fauna of Colombia](13521459.md), [Flora of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flora_of_Colombia), and [Deforestation in Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deforestation_in_Colombia)

Colombia is one of the [megadiverse countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megadiverse_countries) in [biodiversity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biodiversity),[^cite_note-195] ranking first in [bird species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_birds_of_Colombia).[^cite_note-Bird_Species-196] Colombia is the country with the planet's highest biodiversity, having the highest rate of species by area as well as the largest number of [endemisms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endemism) (species that are not found naturally anywhere else) of any country. About 10% of the species of the Earth live in Colombia, including over 1,900 species of bird, more than in Europe and North America combined. Colombia has 10% of the world's [mammals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mammals_of_Colombia) species, 14% of the [amphibian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphibian) species and 18% of the [bird](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endemic_birds_of_Colombia) species of the world.[^cite_note-DIVERSITY_OF_SPECIES_IN_COLOMBIA-197]



The national flower of Colombia, the [endemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endemic) [orchid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orchid) *[Cattleya trianae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cattleya_trianae)*, is named for Colombian botanist and physician [José Jerónimo Triana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Jer%C3%B3nimo_Triana).

[^cite_note-198]

As for plants, the country has between 40,000 and 45,000 plant [species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Species), equivalent to 10 or 20% of total global species, which is even more remarkable given that Colombia is considered a country of intermediate size.[^cite_note-199] Colombia is the second most biodiverse country in the world, lagging only after Brazil which is approximately 7 times bigger.[^cite_note-Biodiversity_of_Colombia-41]

Colombia has about 2,000 species of [marine fish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marine_fish) and is the second most diverse country in [freshwater fish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freshwater_fish). It is also the country with the most endemic species of [butterflies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_butterflies_of_Colombia), is first in [orchid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orchid) species, and has approximately 7,000 species of [beetles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beetle). Colombia is second in the number of amphibian species and is the third most diverse country in [reptiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_reptiles_of_Colombia) and [palms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arecaceae). There are about 1,900 species of [mollusks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mollusk) and according to estimates there are about 300,000 species of [invertebrates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invertebrates) in the country. In Colombia there are 32 terrestrial [biomes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biomes) and 314 types of [ecosystems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecosystems).[^cite_note-200][^cite_note-humboldt-201]

Protected areas and the "National Park System" cover an area of about 14,268,224 hectares (142,682.24 km<sup>2</sup>) and account for 12.77% of the Colombian territory.[^cite_note-202] Compared to neighboring countries, rates of [deforestation in Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deforestation_in_Colombia) are still relatively low.[^cite_note-United_Nations_Deforestation-203] Colombia had a 2018 [Forest Landscape Integrity Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index) mean score of 8.26/10, ranking it 25th globally out of 172 countries.[^cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-204] Colombia is the sixth country in the world by magnitude of total renewable freshwater supply, and still has large reserves of freshwater.[^cite_note-worldwater-205]

### Earthquakes

Colombia has a long and highly active history of earthquakes due to its positioning on the Pacific Ring of Fire.[^cite_note-206]

In the morning of August 10, 2026, a [magnitude 7.4 earthquake struck western Colombia](83931036.md), making history as the strongest recorded earthquake to originate within the country. Its epicenter was near the town of San José del Palmar, roughly 250 miles west of Bogotá. Reportedly at the end of the day there were at least 110 people dead and dozens injured.[^cite_note-207][^cite_note-208]

Other major earthquakes of the century were a 6.2 magnitude quake that killed around 1,200 people on January 25, 1999, and an 8.2 magnitude subduction earthquake offshore near the Colombia-Ecuador border, which generated a destructive tsunami, killing several hundred people along the Pacific coast.[^cite_note-209]

## Government and politics

Main article: [Government of Colombia](76741.md)

See also: [Elections in Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_Colombia) and [Colombian Constitution of 1991](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Constitution_of_1991)



[Abelardo de la Espriella](81665680.md)\
[President](251302.md)



[José Manuel Restrepo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Manuel_Restrepo)\
[Vice President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_President_of_Colombia)



[Casa de Nariño](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casa_de_Nari%C3%B1o) is the official home and principal workplace of the [President of Colombia](251302.md).

The government of Colombia takes place within the framework of a [presidential](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_system) [participatory democratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Participatory_democracy) republic as established in the Constitution of 1991.[^cite_note-Colombian_Constitution_of_1991-158] In accordance with the principle of [separation of powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separation_of_powers), government is divided into three branches: the executive branch, the legislative branch and the judicial branch.[^cite_note-210]

As the head of the executive branch, the [President of Colombia](251302.md) serves as both [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state) and [head of government](37570.md), followed by the [Vice President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_President_of_Colombia) and the [Council of Ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Ministers_of_the_Republic_of_Colombia). The president is elected by popular vote to serve a single four-year term (In 2015, Colombia's Congress approved the repeal of a 2004 constitutional amendment that changed the one-term limit for presidents to a two-term limit).[^cite_note-211] At the provincial level executive power is vested in [department governors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Colombian_Department_Governors), [municipal mayors](3060743.md) and local administrators for smaller administrative subdivisions, such as *[corregimientos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corregimientos_of_Colombia)* or *comunas*.[^cite_note-212] All regional elections are held one year and five months after the presidential election.[^cite_note-Government-213][^cite_note-214]



[Capitolio Nacional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitolio_Nacional), seat of the [Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congress_of_Colombia)

The legislative branch of government is represented nationally by the [Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congress_of_Colombia), a bicameral institution comprising a 166-seat [Chamber of Representatives](645347.md) and a 102-seat [Senate](645242.md).[^cite_note-215][^cite_note-216] The Senate is elected nationally and the Chamber of Representatives is elected in electoral districts.[^cite_note-217] Members of both houses are elected to serve four-year terms two months before the president, also by popular vote.[^cite_note-218]



[Palace of Justice of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Justice_of_Colombia), seat and symbol of the [Judiciary of Colombia](13224622.md)

The judicial branch is headed by [four high courts](13224622.md),[^cite_note-219] consisting of the [Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Justice_of_Colombia) which deals with penal and civil matters, the [Council of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_State_\(Colombia\)), which has special responsibility for [administrative law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_law) and also provides legal advice to the executive, the [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Colombia), responsible for assuring the integrity of the Colombian constitution, and the [Superior Council of Judicature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superior_Council_of_Judicature), responsible for auditing the judicial branch.[^cite_note-220] Colombia operates a system of [civil law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_law_\(legal_system\)), which since 1991 has been applied through an [adversarial system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adversarial_system).[^cite_note-221]

Despite a number of controversies, the [democratic security policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_security_policy) has ensured that former President [Álvaro Uribe](243680.md) remained popular among Colombian people, with his approval rating peaking at 76%, according to a poll in 2009.[^cite_note-222] However, having served two terms, he was constitutionally barred from seeking re-election in 2010.[^cite_note-223] In the run-off [elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Colombian_presidential_election) on 20 June 2010 the former Minister of Defense [Juan Manuel Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Manuel_Santos) won with 69% of the vote against the second most popular candidate, [Antanas Mockus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antanas_Mockus). A second round was required since no candidate received over the 50% winning threshold of votes.[^cite_note-224] Santos won re-election with nearly 51% of the vote in second-round [elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Colombian_presidential_election) on 15 June 2014, beating right-wing rival [Óscar Iván Zuluaga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%93scar_Iv%C3%A1n_Zuluaga), who won 45%.[^cite_note-225] In 2018, [Iván Duque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iv%C3%A1n_Duque) won in the second round of the [election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Colombian_presidential_election) with 54% of the vote, against 42% for his left-wing rival, [Gustavo Petro](13391962.md). His term as Colombia's president ran for four years, beginning on 7 August 2018.[^cite_note-226] In 2022, Colombia [elected](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Colombian_presidential_election) Gustavo Petro, who became its first leftist leader,[^cite_note-227] and [Francia Marquez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francia_Marquez), who was the first black person elected as vice president.[^cite_note-228]

### Administrative divisions

Main articles: [Departments of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Departments_of_Colombia) and [Municipalities of Colombia](3060743.md)

See also: [List of cities in Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Colombia) and [Corregimientos of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corregimientos_of_Colombia)

Colombia is divided into 32 [departments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Departments_of_Colombia) and one [capital district](211271.md), which is treated as a department (Bogotá also serves as the capital of the [department of Cundinamarca](189405.md)). Departments are subdivided into [municipalities](3060743.md), each of which is assigned a municipal seat, and municipalities are in turn subdivided into *[corregimientos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corregimientos_of_Colombia)* in rural areas and into *comunas* in urban areas. Each department has a local government with a governor and assembly directly elected to four-year terms, and each municipality is headed by a mayor and council. There is a popularly elected local administrative board in each of the *corregimientos* or *comunas*.[^cite_note-Administrative_divisions-229][^cite_note-Concerning_the_territorial_organization-230][^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232]

In addition to the capital, four other cities have been designated [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Colombia) (in effect special municipalities), on the basis of special distinguishing features. These are [Barranquilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barranquilla), [Cartagena](63927.md), [Santa Marta](547214.md) and [Buenaventura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buenaventura,_Colombia). Some departments have local administrative subdivisions, where towns have a large concentration of population and municipalities are near each other (for example, in Antioquia and Cundinamarca). Where departments have a low population (for example Amazonas, Vaupés and Vichada), special administrative divisions are employed, such as "department *corregimientos*", which are a hybrid of a municipality and a *corregimiento*.[^cite_note-Administrative_divisions-229][^cite_note-Concerning_the_territorial_organization-230]

Click on a department on the map below to go to its article.
