### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Colombia)

See also: [List of diplomatic missions of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_of_Colombia)



The VII Summit of the [Pacific Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Alliance): Former President of Colombia, [Juan Manuel Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Manuel_Santos) is second from the left.

The foreign affairs of Colombia are headed by the President, as head of state, and managed by the [Minister of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Colombia\)).[^cite_note-The_foreign_affairs-233] Colombia has diplomatic missions in all continents.[^cite_note-234]

Colombia was one of the four founding members of the [Pacific Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Alliance), which is a political, economic and co-operative integration mechanism that promotes the free circulation of goods, services, capital and persons between the members, as well as a common stock exchange and joint embassies in several countries.[^cite_note-The_Pacific_Alliance-235] Colombia is also a member of the United Nations, the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization), the [Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_for_Economic_Co-operation_and_Development), the [Organization of American States](22788.md), the [Organization of Ibero-American States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organization_of_Ibero-American_States), and the [Andean Community of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andean_Community_of_Nations).[^cite_note-236][^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239][^cite_note-240]

Colombia is a global partner of [NATO](21133.md)[^cite_note-241] and a [major non-NATO ally](3812445.md) of the [United States](3434750.md).[^cite_note-\:1-43]

### Military

Main article: [Military Forces of Colombia](5843.md)



[Colombian Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Navy) [Frigate](11464.md) [ARC *Caldas*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Almirante_Padilla-class_frigate)

The executive branch of government is responsible for managing the defense of Colombia, with the President [commander-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander-in-chief) of the armed forces. The [Ministry of Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_National_Defence_\(Colombia\)) exercises day-to-day control of the military and the [Colombian National Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_National_Police). Colombia has 455,461 active military personnel.[^cite_note-242] In 2016, 3.4% of the country's GDP went towards military expenditure, placing it 24th in the world. Colombia's armed forces are the largest in Latin America, and it is the second largest spender on its military after Brazil.[^cite_note-243][^cite_note-244] In 2018, Colombia signed the UN [treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_on_the_Prohibition_of_Nuclear_Weapons).[^cite_note-245]

The Colombian military is divided into three branches: the [National Army of Colombia](2697243.md); the [Colombian Aerospace Force](6719810.md); and the [Colombian Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Navy). The National Police functions as a [gendarmerie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gendarmerie), operating independently from the military as the law enforcement agency for the entire country. Each of these operates with their own intelligence apparatus separate from the [National Intelligence Directorate](53071204.md) (DNI, in Spanish).[^cite_note-The_Colombian_military-246]

The National Army is formed by divisions, brigades, special brigades, and special units,[^cite_note-The_National_Army-247] the Colombian Navy by the [Naval Infantry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Naval_Infantry), the Naval Force of the Caribbean, the Naval Force of the Pacific, the Naval Force of the South, the Naval Force of the East, Colombia Coast Guards, Naval Aviation, and the Specific Command of San Andres y Providencia[^cite_note-the_National_Armada-248] and the Aerospace Force by 15 air units.[^cite_note-The_Air_Force-249]

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Colombia](5839.md)

See also: [Industry of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industry_of_Colombia)



[Skyline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skyline) of [Bogotá](211271.md)'s skyscrapers



Colombia GDP by sector in 2017



[Bancolombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bancolombia) headquarters in [Medellín](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medell%C3%ADn)

Historically an agrarian economy, Colombia urbanized rapidly in the 20th century, by the end of which just 15.8% of the workforce were employed in agriculture, generating just 6.6% of GDP; 20% of the workforce were employed in industry and 65% in services, responsible for 33% and 60% of GDP respectively.[^cite_note-GDP_Composition-250][^cite_note-251] The country's [economic production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_production) is dominated by its strong [domestic demand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domestic_demand). [Consumption expenditure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consumer_spending) by households is the largest component of GDP.[^cite_note-GDPCOLOMBIA-252][^cite_note-GDP-33][^cite_note-Colombianeconomy-253]

Colombia's [market economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_economy) grew steadily in the latter part of the 20th century, with gross domestic product (GDP) increasing at an average rate of over 4% per year between 1970 and 1998. The country suffered a [recession](25382.md) in 1999 (the first full year of negative growth since the [Great Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Depression)), and the recovery was long and painful. However, growth reaching 7% in 2007, one of the [highest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(real\)_growth_rate) in Latin America.[^cite_note-Colombia's_GDP_growth-30] According to [International Monetary Fund](15251.md) estimates, in 2023, Colombia's GDP (PPP) was US\$1 trillion, [32nd in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(PPP\)) and third in South America, after Brazil and Argentina.

Total [government expenditures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_expenditures) account for 28% of the domestic economy. [External debt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/External_debt) equals 40% of gross domestic product. A strong fiscal climate was reaffirmed by a boost in [bond ratings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bond_ratings).[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255][^cite_note-heritage-256] Annual inflation closed 2017 at 4.09% YoY (vs. 5.75% YoY in 2016).[^cite_note-Inflation_Rate-257] The average national [unemployment rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment_rate) in 2017 was 9.4%,[^cite_note-Unemployment_Rate-258] although the informality is the biggest problem facing the [labour market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour_market) (the income of formal workers climbed 24.8% in 5 years while labor incomes of informal workers rose only 9%).[^cite_note-informal_workers-259] Colombia has [free-trade zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free-trade_zone) (FTZ),[^cite_note-FTZ-260] such as Zona Franca del Pacifico, located in the [Valle del Cauca](549733.md), one of the most striking areas for foreign investment.[^cite_note-Zonas_Francas-261]

The [financial sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_sector) has grown favorably due to good liquidity in the economy, the growth of credit and the positive performance of the Colombian economy.[^cite_note-strongmacroeconomicmanagement-34][^cite_note-262][^cite_note-263] The [Colombian Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Stock_Exchange) through the Latin American Integrated Market ([MILA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercado_Integrado_Latinoamericano)) offers a regional market to trade equities.[^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265] Colombia is now one of only three economies with a perfect score on the strength of legal rights index, according to the [World Bank](36752.md).[^cite_note-266]

Colombia is rich in natural resources, and it is heavily dependent on energy and mining exports.[^cite_note-267] Colombia's main exports include mineral fuels, oils, [distillation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distillation) products, fruit and other agricultural products, sugars and sugar confectionery, [food products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_products), plastics, precious stones, metals, forest products, [chemical goods](58721.md), [pharmaceuticals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmaceuticals), vehicles, electronic products, electrical equipment, perfumery and cosmetics, machinery, manufactured articles, textile and fabrics, clothing and footwear, glass and glassware, furniture, prefabricated buildings, military products, home and office material, construction equipment, software, among others.[^cite_note-ITC_Colombia_Exports-268] Principal trading partners are the United States, China, the European Union and some Latin American countries.[^cite_note-Exports-269][^cite_note-Imports-270]

Non-traditional exports have boosted the growth of Colombian foreign sales as well as the diversification of destinations of export thanks to new free [trade agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombia_trade_agreements).[^cite_note-271] Recent economic growth has led to a considerable increase of new millionaires, including the new entrepreneurs, Colombians with a net worth exceeding US\$1 billion.[^cite_note-272][^cite_note-273]

In 2017, however, the National Administrative Department of Statistics (DANE) reported that 26.9% of the population were living below the poverty line, of which 7.4% were in "extreme poverty". The multidimensional poverty rate stands at 17.0 percent of the population.[^cite_note-socio-economic_policies-274] The Government has also been developing a process of [financial inclusion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_inclusion) within the country's most vulnerable population.[^cite_note-275]

The contribution of [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Colombia) to GDP was US\$5,880.3bn (2.0% of total GDP) in 2016. Tourism generated 556,135 jobs (2.5% of total employment) in 2016.[^cite_note-276] Foreign tourist visits were predicted to have risen from 0.6 million in 2007 to 4 million in 2017.[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278]

### Agriculture and natural resources

Main articles: [Agriculture in Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Colombia) and [Mineral industry of Colombia](24504111.md)



[Cerrejón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cerrej%C3%B3n) is an [open-pit coal mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open-pit_coal_mine), the largest of its type, the largest in Latin America and the tenth biggest in the world.

In agriculture, Colombia is one of the five largest producers in the world of [coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee), [avocado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avocado) and [palm oil](57561.md), and one of the 10 largest producers in the world of [sugarcane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugarcane), [banana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banana), [pineapple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pineapple) and [cocoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cocoa_bean). The country also has considerable production of [rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice), [potato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potato) and [cassava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cassava). Although it is not the largest coffee producer in the world (Brazil claims that title), the country has been able to carry out, for decades, a global marketing campaign to add value to the country's product. Colombian palm oil production is one of the most sustainable on the planet, compared to the largest existing producers. Colombia is also among the 20 largest producers in the world of [beef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beef) and [chicken meat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicken_meat).[^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280][^cite_note-281] Colombia is also the 2nd largest [flower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flower) exporter in the world, after the Netherlands.[^cite_note-282] Colombian agriculture emits 55% of Colombia's [greenhouse gas](21350772.md) emissions, mostly from deforestation, over-extensive cattle ranching, land grabbing, and illegal agriculture.[^cite_note-283]

Colombia is an important exporter of [coal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coal) and [petroleum](23195.md) – in 2020, more than 40% of the country's exports were based on these two products.[^cite_note-284] In 2018 it was the 5th largest coal exporter in the world.[^cite_note-285] In 2019, Colombia was the 20th largest petroleum producer in the world, with 791 thousand barrels/day, exporting a good part of its production – the country was the 19th largest oil exporter in the world in 2020.[^cite_note-286] In mining, Colombia is the world's largest producer of [emerald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emerald),[^cite_note-287] and in the production of [gold](12240.md), between 2006 and 2017, the country produced 15 tons per year until 2007, when its production increased significantly, beating the record of 66.1 tons extracted in 2012. In 2017, it extracted 52.2 tons. Currently, the country is among the 25 largest gold producers in the world.[^cite_note-288]

### Energy and transportation

Main articles: [Electricity sector in Colombia](13042135.md) and [Transport in Colombia](5841.md)



[Sogamoso Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sogamoso_Dam)

The electricity production in Colombia comes mainly from [Renewable energy](25784.md) sources. 69.93% is obtained from the [hydroelectric generation](381399.md).[^cite_note-289] Colombia's commitment to renewable energy was recognized in the 2014 *Global Green Economy Index (GGEI)*, ranking among the top 10 nations in the world in terms of greening efficiency sectors.[^cite_note-ggei-290]



Port of [Cartagena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cartagena_de_Indias)

Transportation in Colombia is regulated within the functions of the [Ministry of Transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Transport_\(Colombia\))[^cite_note-MTransport-291] and entities such as the National Roads Institute ([INVÍAS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INV%C3%8DAS)) responsible for the [Highways in Colombia](14006357.md),[^cite_note-292] the [Aerocivil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerocivil), responsible for civil aviation and [airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Colombia),[^cite_note-293] the [National Infrastructure Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Infrastructure_Agency_\(Colombia\)), in charge of [concessions](3261664.md) through [public–private partnerships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public%E2%80%93private_partnership), for the design, construction, maintenance, operation, and administration of the transport infrastructure,[^cite_note-294] the General Maritime Directorate (Dimar) has the responsibility of coordinating maritime traffic control along with the Colombian Navy,[^cite_note-295] among others, and under the supervision of the [Superintendency of Ports and Transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superintendency_of_Ports_and_Transport_\(Colombia\)).[^cite_note-296]

In 2021, Colombia had 204,389 km (127,001 mi) of roads, 32,280 km (20,058 mi) of which were paved. At the end of 2017, the country had around 2,100 km (1,305 mi) of [duplicated highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dual_carriageway).[^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298][^cite_note-transporte-299] [Rail transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Colombia) in Colombia is dedicated almost entirely to [freight shipments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_freight_transport) and the railway network has a length of 1,700 km of potentially active rails.[^cite_note-transporte-299] Colombia has 3,960 kilometers of gas pipelines, 4,900 kilometers of [oil pipelines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_pipelines), and 2,990 kilometers of refined-products pipelines.[^cite_note-transporte-299]

The Colombian government aimed to build 7,000 km of roads between 2016 and 2020, which would reduce travel times by an estimated 30 per cent, and transport costs by an estimated 20 per cent. A toll road concession programme will comprise 40 projects, and is part of a larger strategic goal to invest nearly \$50 bn in transport infrastructure, including railway systems, making the [Magdalena River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magdalena_River) navigable again, improving port facilities, and an expansion of [El Dorado International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Dorado_International_Airport).[^cite_note-300] Colombia is a middle-income country.[^cite_note-301]

### Science and technology

Main article: [Science and technology in Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Colombia)



The [Ministry of Science, Technology and Innovation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Science,_Technology_and_Innovation_\(Colombia\)) (formerly [Colciencias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colciencias)) supports fundamental and applied research in Colombia.

Colombia has more than 3,950 research groups in science and technology.[^cite_note-302] iNNpulsa, a government body that promotes entrepreneurship and innovation in the country, provides grants to startups, in addition to other services it and institutions provide. Colombia was ranked 71st in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025.[^cite_note-303][^cite_note-304] Co-working spaces have arisen to serve as communities for startups large and small.[^cite_note-venturebeat.com-305][^cite_note-306] Organizations such as the Corporation for Biological Research (CIB) for the support of young people interested in scientific work has been successfully developed in Colombia.[^cite_note-307] The [International Center for Tropical Agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Center_for_Tropical_Agriculture) based in Colombia investigates the increasing challenge of [global warming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_warming) and [food security](216361.md).[^cite_note-308]

Important inventions related to medicine have been made in Colombia, such as the first [external artificial pacemaker with internal electrodes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_cardiac_pacemaker), invented by the electrical engineer [Jorge Reynolds Pombo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorge_Reynolds_Pombo), an invention of great importance for those who suffer from heart failure. Also invented in Colombia were the [microkeratome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microkeratome) and keratomileusis technique, which form the fundamental basis of what now is known as [LASIK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LASIK) (one of the most important techniques for the correction of [refractive errors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refractive_error) of vision) and the [Hakim valve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salom%C3%B3n_Hakim#The_Invention_of_the_valve) for the treatment of [hydrocephalus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydrocephalus).[^cite_note-309] Colombia has begun to innovate in military technology for its army and other armies of the world; especially in the design and creation of personal ballistic protection products, military hardware, [military robots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_robot), [bombs](47628.md), simulators and radar.[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311]

Some leading Colombian scientists are Joseph M. Tohme, researcher recognized for his work on the [genetic diversity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genetic_diversity) of food, [Manuel Elkin Patarroyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Elkin_Patarroyo) who is known for his groundbreaking work on [synthetic vaccines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synthetic_vaccine) for [malaria](20423.md), [Francisco Lopera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Lopera) who discovered the "Paisa Mutation" or a type of [early-onset Alzheimer's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early-onset_Alzheimer's_disease),[^cite_note-Francisco_Lopera-312] [Rodolfo Llinás](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodolfo_Llin%C3%A1s) known for his study of the intrinsic [neurons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuron) properties and the theory of a syndrome that had changed the way of understanding the functioning of the brain, Jairo Quiroga Puello recognized for his studies on the characterization of [synthetic substances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synthetic_substance) which can be used to fight fungus, [tumors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tumor), [tuberculosis](30653.md) and even some viruses and Ángela Restrepo who established accurate [diagnoses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_diagnosis) and treatments to combat the effects of a disease caused by *[Paracoccidioides brasiliensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paracoccidioides_brasiliensis)*.[^cite_note-Científicos_colombianos-313][^cite_note-brillantes_colombianos-314][^cite_note-Científicos_destacados-315]

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Colombia)

See also: [List of Colombian departments by population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Colombian_departments_by_population) and [List of cities and towns in Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_Colombia)



[Population density](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_density) of Colombia in 2013



Population history of Colombia

With an estimated 50 million people in 2020, Colombia is the [third-most populous country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_population) in Latin America, after Brazil and Mexico.[^cite_note-PopulationProjections-316] At the beginning of the 20th century, Colombia's population was approximately 4 million.[^cite_note-317] Since the early 1970s, Colombia has experienced steady declines in its fertility, mortality, and population growth rates. The population growth rate for 2016 is estimated to be 0.9%.[^cite_note-Population_growth-318] About 26.8% of the population were 15 years old or younger, 65.7% were between 15 and 64 years old, and 7.4% were over 65 years old. The proportion of older persons in the total population has begun to increase substantially.[^cite_note-319] Colombia is projected to have a population of 55.3 million by 2050.[^cite_note-320]

Estimates for the population of the area that is now Colombia range between 2.5 and 12 million people in 1500; estimates between the extremes include figures of 6[^cite_note-JaramilloUribe1989-68] and 7 million. With the Spanish conquest, the region's population had collapsed to around 1.2 million people in 1600, for an estimated decrease of 52–90%. By the end of the colonial period, it had declined further to around 800,000; it began rising in the early 19th century to around 1.4 million, where it would drop again in the [Colombian War of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_War_of_Independence) to between 1 and 1.2 million. The country's population did not recover to pre-conquest levels until the 1940s, nearly 450 years after its 16th-century peak.[^cite_note-PoblacioCol-321]

The population is concentrated in the [Andean highlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andean_Region_of_Colombia) and along the [Caribbean coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caribbean_Region_of_Colombia), also the population densities are generally higher in the Andean region. The nine eastern lowland departments, comprising about 54% of Colombia's area, have less than 6% of the population.[^cite_note-populationbyregions-190][^cite_note-Population_density-191] Traditionally a rural society, [movement to urban areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbanization) was very heavy in the mid-20th century, and Colombia is now one of the most urbanized countries in Latin America. The urban population increased from 31% of the total in 1938 to nearly 60% in 1973, and by 2014 the figure stood at 76%.[^cite_note-Country_Study-322][^cite_note-323] The population of [Bogotá](211271.md) alone has increased from just over 300,000 in 1938 to approximately 8 million today.[^cite_note-324] In total 72 cities now have populations of 100,000 or more (2015). As of 2012 Colombia has the world's largest populations of [internally displaced persons](427408.md) (IDPs), estimated to be up to 4.9 million people.[^cite_note-UNHCR-325]

The life expectancy was 74.8 years in 2015, and infant mortality was 13.1 per thousand in 2016.[^cite_note-Life_expectancy_at_birth-326][^cite_note-infant_mortality-327] In 2015, 94.58% of adults and 98.66% of youth are literate and the government spends about 4.49% of its GDP on education.[^cite_note-UNESCO-328]

Colombia is a highly urbanized country with 77.1% of the population living in urban areas. The largest cities in the country are [Bogotá](211271.md), with 7,387,400 inhabitants, [Medellín](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medell%C3%ADn), with 2,382,399 inhabitants, [Cali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cali), with 2,172,527 inhabitants, and [Barranquilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barranquilla), with 1,205,284 inhabitants.[^cite_note-329]
