### Ethnic groups

Main article: [Race and ethnicity in Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Race_and_ethnicity_in_Colombia)

Racial groups in Colombia ([2018 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Colombian_census)):

[^cite_note-Grupos_étnicos-331]

1. Undeclared (85.0%)

2. [Blacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Colombians) (9.34%)

3. [Indigenous](4053513.md) (4.31%)

4. [Romanis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_people_in_Colombia) (0.01%)

5. Not specified (1.35%)

Colombia is ethnically diverse, its people descending from the original [Native](4053513.md) inhabitants, Spanish conquistadors, [Africans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Colombian) originally brought to the country as slaves, and 20th-century [immigrants from Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_diaspora) and the [Middle East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Colombians), all contributing to a diverse cultural heritage.[^cite_note-Colombia_is_ethnically_diverse-332] The demographic distribution reflects a pattern that is influenced by colonial history.[^cite_note-333] Whites live all throughout the country, mainly in urban centers and the burgeoning highland and coastal cities. The populations of the major cities also include mestizos. [Mestizo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mestizo_Colombian) *campesinos* (people living in rural areas) also live in the Andean highlands where some Spanish conquerors mixed with the women of Amerindian [chiefdoms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiefdoms). Mestizos include artisans and small tradesmen that have played a major part in the urban expansion of recent decades.[^cite_note-334][^cite_note-grupos_étnicos-5] In a study by the American Journal of Physical Anthropology, Colombians have an average ancestry of 47% Amerindian DNA, 42% European DNA, and 11% African DNA.[^cite_note-Rojas2010-335]

The [2018 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Colombian_Census) reported that the "non-ethnic population", consisting of [whites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Colombian) and mestizos (those of mixed European and Amerindian ancestry), constituted 87.6% of the national population. 6.7% is of [African](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afro-Colombian) ancestry. [Indigenous Amerindians](4053513.md) constitute 4.3% of the population. [Raizal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raizal) people constitute 0.06% of the population. [Palenquero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palenquero) people constitute 0.02% of the population. 0.01% of the population are [Roma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_people). A study by Latinobarómetro in 2023 estimates that 50.3% of the population are [Mestizo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mestizo_Colombians), 26.4% are [White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Colombians), 9.5% are [Indigenous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_Colombia), 9.0% are [Black](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afro-Colombians), 4.4% are [Mulatto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mulatto), and 0.4% are [Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Colombians), these estimates would equate to around 26 million people being Mestizo, 14 million being White, 5 million being Indigenous, 5 million being Black, 2 million being Mulatto, and 200k being Asian.[^cite_note-2023est-336]&#x20;

Ethnic groups of Colombia according to Latinobarómetro 2023:

[^cite_note-2023est-336]

1. [Mestizo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mestizo_Colombians) (50.3%)

2. [White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Colombians) (26.4%)

3. [Amerindian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_Colombia) (9.50%)

4. [Black](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Colombians) (9.00%)

5. [Mulatto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mulatto) (4.40%)

6. [East Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Colombians) (0.40%)

The Federal Research Division estimated that the 86% of the population that did not consider themselves part of one of the ethnic groups indicated by the 2006 census was divided into 49% Mestizo or of mixed European and Amerindian ancestry, and 37% White, mainly of Spanish lineage, but there is also a large population of Middle East descent; in some sectors of society there is a considerable input of German and Italian ancestry.[^cite_note-The_Society_and_Its_Environment-337][^cite_note-grupos_étnicos-5]

Many of the [Indigenous peoples](4053513.md) experienced a reduction in population during the Spanish rule[^cite_note-338] and many others were absorbed into the mestizo population, but the remainder currently represents over eighty distinct cultures. Reserves (*resguardos*) established for indigenous peoples occupy 30,571,640 hectares (305,716.4 km<sup>2</sup>) (27% of the country's total) and are inhabited by more than 800,000 people.[^cite_note-Resguardos_Indígenas-339] Some of the largest indigenous groups are the [Wayuu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayuu),[^cite_note-wayuu-340] the [Paez](2478404.md), the Pastos, the [Emberá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ember%C3%A1_people) and the [Zenú](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zen%C3%BA).[^cite_note-pueblos_indígenas-341] The departments of [La Guajira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_La_Guajira), [Cauca](549662.md), [Nariño](549702.md), [Córdoba](549670.md) and [Sucre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sucre_Department) have the largest indigenous populations.[^cite_note-grupos_étnicos-5]

The [Organización Nacional Indígena de Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Indigenous_Organization_of_Colombia) (ONIC), founded at the first National Indigenous Congress in 1982, is an organization representing the indigenous peoples of Colombia. In 1991, Colombia signed and ratified the current international law concerning indigenous peoples, [Indigenous and Tribal Peoples Convention, 1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_and_Tribal_Peoples_Convention,_1989).[^cite_note-Ratifications_-_ilo.org-342]

[Sub-Saharan Africans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-Saharan_Africans) were brought as [slaves](49005.md), mostly to the coastal lowlands, beginning early in the 16th century and continuing into the 19th century. Large Afro-Colombian communities are found today on the Pacific Coast.[^cite_note-343] Numerous [Jamaicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamaican_people) migrated mainly to the islands of San Andres and Providencia. A number of other Europeans and North Americans migrated to the country in the late 19th and early 20th centuries, including people from the former [USSR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) during and after the [Second World War](32927.md).[^cite_note-Extranjeros_en_Colombia-344][^cite_note-Migraciones_Internacionales-345]

Many immigrant communities have settled on the Caribbean coast, in particular recent immigrants from the [Middle East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_West_Asia) and [Europe](9239.md). Barranquilla (the largest city of the Colombian Caribbean) and other Caribbean cities have the largest populations of [Lebanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Colombian), [Palestinian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_people), and other [Levantines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Colombians).[^cite_note-arab_colombians-346][^cite_note-347] There are also important communities of [Romanis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_people) and [Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Colombia).[^cite_note-Colombia_is_ethnically_diverse-332] There is a major migration trend of [Venezuelans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_people), due to the political and economic situation in Venezuela.[^cite_note-348] In August 2019, Colombia offered citizenship to more than 24,000 children of Venezuelan refugees who were born in Colombia.[^cite_note-349]

### Languages

Main article: [Languages of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Colombia)

See also: [Colombian Spanish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Spanish)

Around 99.2% of Colombians speak Spanish, also called Castilian; 65 [Amerindian languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amerindian_language), two [Creole languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creole_languages), the [Romani language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_language) and [Colombian Sign Language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Sign_Language) are also used in the country. [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language) has official status in the [archipelago of San Andrés, Providencia and Santa Catalina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archipelago_of_San_Andr%C3%A9s,_Providencia_and_Santa_Catalina).[^cite_note-LEY47DE1993-3][^cite_note-lenguas_indígenas-350][^cite_note-Languages_of_Colombia-351][^cite_note-MapofthelanguagesofColombia-352]

Including Spanish, a total of 101 languages are listed for Colombia in the [Ethnologue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnologue) database. The specific number of spoken languages varies slightly since some authors consider as different languages what others consider to be varieties or dialects of the same language. Best estimates recorded 71 languages that are spoken in-country today – most of which belong to the [Chibchan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chibchan_languages), [Tucanoan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tucanoan_languages), [Bora–Witoto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bora%E2%80%93Witoto_languages), [Guajiboan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guajiboan_languages), [Arawakan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arawakan_languages), [Cariban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cariban_languages), [Barbacoan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbacoan_languages), and [Saliban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piaroa%E2%80%93Saliban_languages) language families. There are currently more than 850,000 speakers of native languages.[^cite_note-ethnologue-353][^cite_note-nativelanguages-354]

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Colombia)

See also: [Freedom of religion in Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_religion_in_Colombia)



The [Las Lajas Sanctuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Lajas_Sanctuary) in the southern Colombian Department of [Nariño](549702.md)

The [National Administrative Department of Statistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Administrative_Department_of_Statistics) (DANE) does not collect religious statistics, and accurate reports are difficult to obtain. However, based on various studies and a survey, about 90% of the population adheres to [Christianity](5211.md), the majority of which (70.9%–79%) are [Roman Catholic](606848.md), while a significant minority (16.7%) adhere to [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism) (primarily [Evangelicalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism)). Some 4.7% of the population is [atheist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheist) or agnostic, while 3.5% claim to believe in God but do not follow a specific religion. 1.8% of Colombians adhere to [Jehovah's Witnesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jehovah's_Witnesses) and [Adventism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adventism) and less than 1% adhere to other religions, such as the [Baháʼí Faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith), [Islam](6037917.md), [Judaism](15624.md), [Buddhism](3267529.md), [Mormonism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mormonism), [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism), [Indigenous religions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animism), [Hare Krishna movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hare_Krishna_movement), [Rastafari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rastafari) movement, [Eastern Orthodoxy](10186.md), and spiritual studies. The remaining people either did not respond or replied that they did not know. In addition to the above statistics, 35.9% of Colombians reported that they did not practice their faith actively.[^cite_note-Religion-355][^cite_note-Religion2-356][^cite_note-Religion_in_Latin_America-357] 1,519,562 people in Colombia, or around 3% of the population reported following an Indigenous religion.

While Colombia remains a mostly Roman Catholic country by [baptism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptism) numbers, the 1991 Colombian constitution guarantees freedom of religion and all religious faiths and churches are equally free before the law.[^cite_note-358]

### Education

Main article: [Education in Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Colombia)

The educational experience of many Colombian children begins with attendance at a [preschool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preschool) academy until age five (*Educación preescolar*). Basic education (*Educación básica*) is compulsory by law.[^cite_note-359] It has two stages: Primary basic education (*Educación básica primaria*) which goes from first to fifth grade – children from six to ten years old, and Secondary basic education (*Educación básica secundaria*), which goes from sixth to ninth grade. Basic education is followed by Middle vocational education (*Educación media vocacional*) that comprises the tenth and eleventh grades. It may have different vocational training modalities or specialties (academic, technical, business, and so on.) according to the curriculum adopted by each school.[^cite_note-sistema_educativo-360]



M5 building – [National University of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_University_of_Colombia), designed by [Pedro Nel Gómez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedro_Nel_G%C3%B3mez)

After the successful completion of all the basic and middle education years, a [high-school diploma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-school_diploma) is awarded. The high-school graduate is known as a *bachiller*, because secondary basic school and middle education are traditionally considered together as a unit called *bachillerato* (sixth to eleventh grade). Students in their final year of middle education take the [ICFES test](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ICFES_Examination) (now renamed Saber 11) to gain access to higher education (*Educación superior*). This higher education includes undergraduate professional studies, technical, technological and intermediate professional education, and post-graduate studies. Technical professional institutions of Higher Education are also opened to students holder of a qualification in Arts and Business. This qualification is usually awarded by the [SENA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Service_of_Learning_\(Colombia\)) after a two years curriculum.[^cite_note-361]

*Bachilleres* (high-school graduates) may enter into a professional undergraduate career program offered by a university; these programs last up to five years (or less for technical, technological and intermediate professional education, and post-graduate studies), even as much to six to seven years for some careers, such as medicine. In Colombia, there is not an institution such as college; students go directly into a career program at a university or any other educational institution to obtain a professional, technical or technological title. Once graduated from the university, people are granted a (professional, technical or technological) diploma and licensed (if required) to practice the career they have chosen. For some professional career programs, students are required to take the Saber-Pro test, in their final year of undergraduate academic education.[^cite_note-sistema_educativo-360]

Public spending on education as a proportion of gross domestic product in 2015 was 4.49%. This represented 15.05% of total government expenditure. The [primary and secondary gross enrolment ratios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_enrolment_ratio) stood at 113.56% and 98.09% respectively. [School-life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School-life_expectancy) was 14.42 years. A total of 94.58% of the population aged 15 and older were recorded as literate, including 98.66% of those aged 15–24.[^cite_note-UNESCO-328]

### Health

Main article: [Health care in Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_care_in_Colombia)



Colombia leads the annual *[América Economía](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Am%C3%A9rica_Econom%C3%ADa)* ranking of the best clinics and hospitals in Latin America.

[^cite_note-Colombian_clinics-362]

The overall [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy) in Colombia at birth is 79.3 years (76.7 years for males and 81.9 years for females).[^cite_note-Life_expectancy_at_birth-326] Healthcare reforms have led to massive improvements in the healthcare systems of the country, with health standards in Colombia improving very much since the 1980s. The new system has widened population coverage by the social and health security system from 21% (pre-1993) to 96% in 2012.[^cite_note-363] In 2017, the government declared a [cancer research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cancer_research) and treatment center as a Project of National Strategic Interest.[^cite_note-364]

A 2016 study conducted by *[América Economía](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Am%C3%A9rica_Econom%C3%ADa)* magazine ranked 21 Colombian [health care institutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_facility) among the top 44 in Latin America, amounting to 48 percent of the total.[^cite_note-Colombian_clinics-362] In 2022, 26 Colombian hospitals were among the 61 best in [Latin America](18524.md) (42% total).[^cite_note-365] Also in 2023, two Colombian hospitals were among the top 75 of the world.[^cite_note-366][^cite_note-367]

### Crime



[Colombian National Police Special Operations Command (COPES)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_National_Police_Special_Operations_Command), displayed in [Pereira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pereira,_Colombia). A subdivision of the [National Police](7278792.md) for the fight against organized crime and terrorist acts.

This section is an excerpt from [Crime in Colombia](33726346.md).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Crime_in_Colombia\&action=edit)]



Homicide rate in Colombia (1995-2015)

Colombia has a high crime rate due to being a center for the cultivation and trafficking of [cocaine](7701.md). The [Colombian conflict](1857628.md) began in the mid-1960s and is a [low-intensity conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Low-intensity_conflict) between [Colombian governments](76741.md), [paramilitary groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramilitarism_in_Colombia), [crime syndicates](13005346.md), and left-wing guerrillas such as the [Revolutionary Armed Forces of Colombia](26100.md) (FARC), and the [National Liberation Army](822032.md) (ELN), fighting each other to increase their influence in Colombian territory. Two of the most important international factors that have contributed to the Colombian conflict are multinational companies and the United States.[^cite_note-Crime_in_Colombia_HistoricalCommission-368][^cite_note-Crime_in_Colombia_colombia-and-us-54-369][^cite_note-Crime_in_Colombia_International_actors-370]

Elements of all the armed groups have been involved in [drug trafficking](218449.md). In a country where state capacity has been weak in some regions, the result has been a grinding war on multiple fronts, with the [civilian](145804.md) population caught in between and often deliberately targeted for "collaborating". Human rights advocates blame paramilitaries for massacres, "disappearances", and cases of [torture](47702.md) and [forced displacement](477535.md). Rebel groups are behind assassinations, kidnapping and extortion.[^cite_note-371]

In 2011, [President](251302.md) [Juan Manuel Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Manuel_Santos) launched the "Borders for Prosperity" plan[^cite_note-Crime_in_Colombia_\:0-372] to fight poverty and combat violence from illegal armed groups along Colombia's borders through social and economic development.[^cite_note-Crime_in_Colombia_\:1-373] The plan received praise from the [International Crisis Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Crisis_Group).[^cite_note-Crime_in_Colombia_icgrpt-374] Colombia registered a [homicide](123451.md) rate of 24.4 per 100,000 in 2016, the lowest since 1974. The 40-year low in murders came the same year the [government](76741.md) signed a [peace agreement](51422824.md) with the FARC.[^cite_note-Crime_in_Colombia_auto-375] The murder rate further decreased to 22.6 in 2020, though still among the highest in the world, it decreased 73% from 84 in 1991. In the 1980s and 1990s it regularly ranked as number one.

Since the beginning of the [crisis in Venezuela](52966258.md) and the mass [emigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emigration) of [Venezuelans](19835614.md) during the [Venezuelan refugee crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_refugee_crisis), desperate Venezuelans have been recruited into [gangs](80408.md) in order to survive by other Venezuelan gang members.[^cite_note-Crime_in_Colombia_\:02-376] Venezuelan women have also resorted to [prostitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prostitution_in_Colombia) in order to make a living in Colombia.[^cite_note-Crime_in_Colombia_\:02-376]

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Colombia)

See also: [List of festivals in Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_festivals_in_Colombia) and [Colombian folklore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_folklore)

Colombia lies at the crossroads of [Latin America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_American_culture) and the broader region of the Americas, and as such has been hit by a wide range of cultural influences. [Native American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas), [Spanish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Spain) and other [European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Europe), [African](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Africa), [American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_the_United_States), [Caribbean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_the_Caribbean), and [Middle Eastern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_culture) influences, as well as other Latin American cultural influences, are all present in Colombia's modern culture. Urban migration, industrialization, globalization, and other political, social and economic changes have also left an impression.

Many [national symbols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_symbols_of_Colombia), both objects and themes, have arisen from Colombia's diverse cultural traditions and aim to represent what Colombia, and the Colombian people, have in common. Cultural expressions in Colombia are promoted by the government through the [Ministry of Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Culture_\(Colombia\)).[^cite_note-377]

### Visual arts

Main article: [Colombian art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_art)



Work by the painter and sculptor [Fernando Botero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Botero)



[The Virgin of Chiquinquirá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Lady_of_the_Rosary_of_Chiquinquir%C3%A1) (1562) by [Alonso de Narváez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alonso_de_Narv%C3%A1ez).

[^cite_note-378]

[^cite_note-379]

&#x20;She is the Catholic [Patroness](68055.md) of Colombia. The original canvas is located in the [Basilica of Our Lady of the Rosary of Chiquinquirá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basilica_of_Our_Lady_of_the_Rosary_of_Chiquinquir%C3%A1).



Mural by [Santiago Martínez Delgado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santiago_Mart%C3%ADnez_Delgado)

Colombian art has over 3,000 years of history. Colombian artists have captured the country's changing political and cultural backdrop using a range of styles and mediums. There is archeological evidence of ceramics being produced earlier in Colombia than anywhere else in the Americas, dating as early as 3,000 BCE.[^cite_note-donquijote.org-380][^cite_note-artecolombiano-381]

The earliest examples of gold craftsmanship have been attributed to the Tumaco people[^cite_note-382] of the Pacific coast and date to around 325 BCE. Roughly between 200 BCE and 800 CE, the [San Agustín culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Agust%C3%ADn,_Huila), masters of [stonecutting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stonemasonry), entered its "classical period". They erected raised [ceremonial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceremony) centers, [sarcophagi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarcophagi), and large stone [monoliths](318257.md) depicting [anthropomorphic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthropomorphic) and [zoomorphic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoomorphism) forms out of [stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_\(geology\)).[^cite_note-artecolombiano-381][^cite_note-San_Agustín-383]

Colombian art has followed the trends of the time, so during the 16th to 18th centuries, [Spanish Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholicism_in_Spain) had a huge influence on Colombian art, and the popular [baroque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baroque) style was replaced with [rococo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rococo) when the Bourbons ascended to the [Spanish crown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Spain).[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-385] During this era, as a Spanish colony, the most important painters were [Gregorio Vásquez de Arce y Ceballos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregorio_V%C3%A1squez_de_Arce_y_Ceballos), [Gaspar de Figueroa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaspar_de_Figueroa), [Baltasar Vargas de Figueroa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltasar_Vargas_de_Figueroa), [Baltasar de Figueroa the Elder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltasar_de_Figueroa_\(the_Elder\)), [Antonio Acero de la Cruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Acero_de_la_Cruz) and [Joaquín Gutiérrez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joaqu%C3%ADn_Guti%C3%A9rrez_\(painter\)?action=edit\&redlink=1), of which their works are preserved. Also important was [Alonso de Narváez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alonso_de_Narv%C3%A1ez) who, although born in the province of Seville, spent most of his life in colonial Colombia, also the Italian [Angelino Medoro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angelino_Medoro), lived in Colombia and Peru, and left works of art preserved in several churches in [Tunja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tunja) city.

During the mid-19th century, one of the most remarkable painters was [Ramón Torres Méndez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ram%C3%B3n_Torres_M%C3%A9ndez), who produced a series of good quality paintings depicting the people and their customs of different Colombian regions. Also noteworthy in the 19th century were [Andrés de Santa María](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9s_de_Santa_Maria), [Pedro José Figueroa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedro_Jos%C3%A9_Figueroa), [Epifanio Garay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epifanio_Garay?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Mercedes Delgado Mallarino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes_Delgado_Mallarino?action=edit\&redlink=1), [José María Espinosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Mar%C3%ADa_Espinosa?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Ricardo Acevedo Bernal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ricardo_Acevedo_Bernal), between many others.

More recently, Colombian artists [Pedro Nel Gómez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedro_Nel_G%C3%B3mez) and [Santiago Martínez Delgado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santiago_Mart%C3%ADnez_Delgado) started the Colombian Murial Movement in the 1940s, featuring the [neoclassical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoclassicism) features of [Art Deco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Deco).[^cite_note-donquijote.org-380][^cite_note-artecolombiano-381][^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387] Since the 1950s, the Colombian art started to have a distinctive point of view, reinventing traditional elements under the concepts of the 20th century. Examples of this are the Greiff [portraits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portrait) by [Ignacio Gómez Jaramillo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ignacio_G%C3%B3mez_Jaramillo), showing what the Colombian art could do with the new techniques applied to typical Colombian themes. Carlos Correa, with his [paradigmatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paradigm) "Naturaleza muerta en silencio" (silent dead nature), combines geometrical [abstraction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abstraction_\(art\)) and [cubism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cubism). [Alejandro Obregón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alejandro_Obreg%C3%B3n) is often considered as the father of modern Colombian painting, and one of the most influential artist in this period, due to his originality, the painting of Colombian landscapes with [symbolic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symbolism_\(arts\)) and [expressionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expressionism) use of animals, (specially the [Andean condor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andean_condor)).[^cite_note-artecolombiano-381][^cite_note-388][^cite_note-389] [Fernando Botero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Botero), [Omar Rayo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omar_Rayo), [Enrique Grau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrique_Grau), [Édgar Negret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89dgar_Negret), [David Manzur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Manzur), [Rodrigo Arenas Betancourt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodrigo_Arenas_Betancourt), [Oscar Murillo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Murillo_\(artist\)), [Doris Salcedo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doris_Salcedo) and [Oscar Muñoz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Mu%C3%B1oz_\(artist\)) are some of the Colombian artists featured at the international level.[^cite_note-donquijote.org-380][^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391][^cite_note-museorayo-392]

The Colombian sculpture from the sixteenth to 18th centuries was mostly devoted to [religious depictions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_art) of ecclesiastic art, strongly influenced by the Spanish schools of [sacred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacred) sculpture. During the early period of the Colombian republic, the national artists were focused in the production of sculptural portraits of politicians and public figures, in a plain [neoclassicist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoclassicist) trend.[^cite_note-393] During the 20th century, the Colombian sculpture began to develop a bold and innovative work with the aim of reaching a better understanding of national sensitivity.[^cite_note-artecolombiano-381][^cite_note-Colombian_sculptors-394]

Colombian photography was marked by the arrival of the [daguerreotype](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daguerreotype). [Jean-Baptiste Louis Gros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Baptiste_Louis_Gros) was who brought the daguerreotype process to Colombia in 1841. The Piloto public library has Latin America's largest archive of negatives, containing 1.7 million antique photographs covering Colombia 1848 until 2005.[^cite_note-395][^cite_note-396]

The Colombian press has promoted the work of the [cartoonists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cartoonist). In recent decades, [fanzines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fanzine), internet and [independent publishers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_publisher) have been fundamental to the growth of the comic in Colombia.[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-398][^cite_note-399]

### Architecture

Main article: [Architecture of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Colombia)

See also: [Muisca architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muisca_architecture)

Throughout the times, there have been a variety of [architectural styles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_architectural_styles), from those of indigenous peoples to contemporary ones, passing through colonial (military and religious), Republican, transition and modern styles.[^cite_note-400]



Colonial balconies in the streets of [Cartagena](63927.md)



Colonial [Popayán](657155.md) main plaza, [Cauca Department](549662.md)



Colonial [Villa de Leyva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villa_de_Leyva), [Boyacá Department](185665.md)

Ancient habitation areas, longhouses, [crop terraces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrace_\(agriculture\)), roads as the [Inca road system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inca_road_system), cemeteries, [hypogeums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypogeum) and [necropolises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Necropolis) are all part of the [architectural heritage of indigenous peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_architecture).[^cite_note-401] Some prominent indigenous structures are the [preceramic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andean_preceramic) and [ceramic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceramic) archaeological site of [Tequendama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tequendama),[^cite_note-402] [Tierradentro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tierradentro) (a park that contains the largest concentration of [pre-Columbian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_pre-Columbian_cultures) monumental [shaft tombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaft_tomb) with side chambers),[^cite_note-403] the largest collection of religious monuments and [megalithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megalithic) sculptures in South America, located in [San Agustín, Huila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Agust%C3%ADn_Archaeological_Park),[^cite_note-San_Agustín-383][^cite_note-404] [Lost city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ciudad_Perdida) (an archaeological site with a series of terraces carved into the mountainside, a net of tiled roads, and several circular plazas), and the large villages mainly built with [stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stone), wood, cane, and mud.[^cite_note-405] Architecture during the period of conquest and colonization is mainly derived of adapting [European styles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_architecture) to local conditions, and [Spanish influence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_architecture), especially [Andalusian](2736.md) and [Extremaduran](68204.md), can be easily seen.[^cite_note-406] When Europeans founded cities two things were making simultaneously: the dimensioning of geometrical space ([town square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Town_square), street), and the location of a tangible point of [orientation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Map_orientation).[^cite_note-407] The construction of [forts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort) was common throughout the Caribbean and in some cities of the interior, because of the dangers posed to Spanish colonial settlements from English, French and Dutch [pirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piracy_in_the_Caribbean) and hostile indigenous groups.[^cite_note-408] Churches, chapels, schools, and hospitals belonging to [religious orders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_religious_order) have a great urban influence.[^cite_note-1550–1750-409] [Baroque architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baroque_architecture) is used in military buildings and public spaces.[^cite_note-410] Marcelino Arroyo, [Francisco José de Caldas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Jos%C3%A9_de_Caldas) and Domingo de Petrés were great representatives of [neo-classical architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-classical_architecture).[^cite_note-1550–1750-409]

The [National Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitolio_Nacional) is a great representative of romanticism.[^cite_note-411] Wood was extensively used in doors, windows, railings, and ceilings during the colonization of [Antioquia](185251.md). The [Caribbean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caribbean_region_of_Colombia) architecture acquires a strong [Arabic influence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_architecture).[^cite_note-412] The [Teatro Colón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teatro_de_Crist%C3%B3bal_Col%C3%B3n) in Bogotá is a lavish example of architecture from the 19th century.[^cite_note-413] The quintas houses with innovations in the [volumetric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volumetric) conception are some of the best examples of the Republican architecture; the Republican action in the city focused on the design of three types of [spaces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spatial_analysis): parks with forests, small [urban parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_park) and [avenues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avenue_\(landscape\)) and the [Gothic style](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gothic_architecture) was most commonly used for the design of churches.[^cite_note-414]

Deco style, [modern neoclassicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rationalism_\(architecture\)), [eclecticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eclecticism_in_architecture) [folklorist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklorist) and [art deco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_deco) [ornamental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ornament_\(art\)) resources significantly influenced the architecture of Colombia, especially during the transition period.[^cite_note-415] [Modernism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Style_\(architecture\)) contributed with new construction technologies and new [materials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Building_material) (steel, [reinforced concrete](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reinforced_concrete), glass and synthetic materials) and the [topology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Topology) architecture and [lightened slabs system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concrete_slab) also have a great influence.[^cite_note-416] The most influential architects of the modern movement were [Rogelio Salmona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rogelio_Salmona) and Fernando Martínez Sanabria.[^cite_note-417]

The [contemporary architecture](3970948.md) of Colombia is designed to give greater importance to the [materials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Building_material), this architecture takes into account the specific [natural and artificial geographies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geographical_feature) and is also an architecture that appeals to the [senses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sense).[^cite_note-418] The [conservation of the architectural and urban heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architectural_conservation) of Colombia has been promoted in recent years.[^cite_note-419]

### Literature

Main article: [Colombian literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_literature)



The [Nobel literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Nobel_laureates_in_Literature) prize winner [Gabriel García Márquez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_Garc%C3%ADa_M%C3%A1rquez)

[^cite_note-420]

Colombian literature dates back to pre-Columbian era; a notable example of the period is the epic poem known as the *Legend of Yurupary*.[^cite_note-The_Legend_of_Yurupary-421] In Spanish colonial times, notable writers include [Juan de Castellanos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_de_Castellanos) (*[Elegías de varones ilustres de Indias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eleg%C3%ADas_de_varones_ilustres_de_Indias)*), Hernando Domínguez Camargo and his epic poem to San Ignacio de Loyola, [Pedro Simón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedro_Sim%C3%B3n) and [Juan Rodríguez Freyle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Rodr%C3%ADguez_Freyle).[^cite_note-422]

Post-independence literature linked to Romanticism highlighted [Antonio Nariño](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Nari%C3%B1o), [José Fernández Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Fern%C3%A1ndez_Madrid), [Camilo Torres Tenorio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camilo_Torres_Tenorio) and [Francisco Antonio Zea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Antonio_Zea).[^cite_note-423][^cite_note-424] In the second half of the nineteenth century and early twentieth century the literary genre known as *[costumbrismo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costumbrismo)* became popular; great writers of this period were [Tomás Carrasquilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom%C3%A1s_Carrasquilla), [Jorge Isaacs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorge_Isaacs) and [Rafael Pombo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Pombo) (the latter of whom wrote notable works of children's literature).[^cite_note-425][^cite_note-426] Within that period, authors such as [José Asunción Silva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Asunci%C3%B3n_Silva), [José Eustasio Rivera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Eustasio_Rivera), [León de Greiff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le%C3%B3n_de_Greiff), [Porfirio Barba-Jacob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porfirio_Barba-Jacob) and [José María Vargas Vila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Mar%C3%ADa_Vargas_Vila) developed the [modernist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modernismo) movement.[^cite_note-427][^cite_note-428][^cite_note-429] In 1872, Colombia established the [Colombian Academy of Language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Academy_of_Language), the first Spanish language academy in the Americas.[^cite_note-430] [Candelario Obeso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candelario_Obeso) wrote the groundbreaking *Cantos Populares de mi Tierra* (1877), the first book of poetry by an Afro-Colombian author.[^cite_note-431][^cite_note-Candelario_Obeso-432]

Between 1939 and 1940 seven books of poetry were published under the name *[Stone and Sky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stone_and_Sky_\(movement\))* in the city of Bogotá that significantly influenced the country; they were edited by the poet Jorge Rojas.[^cite_note-433] In the following decade, [Gonzalo Arango](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gonzalo_Arango) founded the movement of "[nothingness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nadaism)" in response to the violence of the time;[^cite_note-434] he was influenced by [nihilism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nihilism), [existentialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Existentialism), and the thought of another great Colombian writer: [Fernando González Ochoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Gonz%C3%A1lez_\(writer\)).[^cite_note-435] During the [boom in Latin American literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_American_Boom), successful writers emerged, led by [Nobel laureate](23385442.md) [Gabriel García Márquez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_Garc%C3%ADa_M%C3%A1rquez) and his magnum opus, *[One Hundred Years of Solitude](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Hundred_Years_of_Solitude)*, [Eduardo Caballero Calderón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduardo_Caballero_Calder%C3%B3n), [Manuel Mejía Vallejo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Mej%C3%ADa_Vallejo), and [Álvaro Mutis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81lvaro_Mutis), a writer who was awarded the [Cervantes Prize](3930514.md) and the [Prince of Asturias Award for Letters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_of_Asturias_Award_for_Letters).[^cite_note-436][^cite_note-437]

### Music

Main article: [Music of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Colombia)

Colombia has a vibrant collage of talent that touches a full spectrum of [rhythms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhythm). It is known as the land of a thousand rhythms, at around 1,024 folk rhythms. Musicians, composers, music producers and singers from Colombia are recognized internationally such as [Shakira](6479315.md), [Juanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juanes), [Carlos Vives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Vives) and others.[^cite_note-438] Colombian music blends European-influenced guitar and song structure with large [gaita flutes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaita_flutes) and percussion instruments from the indigenous population, while its percussion structure and dance forms come from Africa. Colombia has a diverse and dynamic musical environment.[^cite_note-about.com-439]



Regions of Colombia by their traditional music

[Guillermo Uribe Holguín](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guillermo_Uribe_Holgu%C3%ADn), an important cultural figure in the [National Symphony Orchestra of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Symphony_Orchestra_of_Colombia), [Luis Antonio Calvo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Antonio_Calvo) and Blas Emilio Atehortúa are some of the greatest exponents of the [art music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_music).[^cite_note-440] The [Bogotá Philharmonic Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bogot%C3%A1_Philharmonic_Orchestra) is one of the most active orchestras in Colombia.[^cite_note-441]

Caribbean music has many vibrant rhythms, such as [cumbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumbia_\(Colombia\)) (it is played by the [maracas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maraca), the drums, the gaitas and [guacharaca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guacharaca)), [porro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porro) (it is a monotonous but joyful rhythm), [mapalé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mapal%C3%A9) (with its fast rhythm and constant [clapping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clapping)) and the "[vallenato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vallenato)", which originated in the northern part of the [Caribbean](18956035.md) coast (the rhythm is mainly played by the caja, the guacharaca, and [accordion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accordion)).[^cite_note-Colombian_music-442][^cite_note-443][^cite_note-444][^cite_note-445][^cite_note-446]

The music from the [Pacific coast](751843.md), such as the [currulao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Currulao_\(music_genre\)?action=edit\&redlink=1), is characterized by its strong use of drums (instruments such as the native [marimba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marimba), the conunos, the [bass drum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bass_drum), the [side drum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Side_drum), and the cuatro guasas or tubular rattle). An important rhythm of the south region of the Pacific coast is the [contradanza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contradanza) (it is used in dance shows due to the striking colours of the costumes).[^cite_note-Colombian_music-442][^cite_note-447][^cite_note-448] Marimba music, traditional chants and dances from the Colombia South Pacific region are on [UNESCO's Representative List of the Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_Intangible_Cultural_Heritage_Lists).[^cite_note-449][^cite_note-450][^cite_note-451]



[Jorge Celedón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorge_Celed%C3%B3n) of the [Binomio de Oro de América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Binomio_de_Oro_de_Am%C3%A9rica) band. The [Vallenato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vallenato), along with [Cumbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumbia_\(Colombia\)), are the two most popular Colombian folk music genres heard in Latin America.

Important musical rhythms of the [Andean Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andean_Region_\(Colombia\)) are the [danza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danza) (dance of Andean folklore arising from the transformation of the European contredance), the [bambuco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bambuco) (it is played with guitar, [tiple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_tiple)[^cite_note-452] and [mandolin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandolin), the rhythm is danced by couples), the [pasillo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pasillo) (a rhythm inspired by the Austrian [waltz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waltz) and the Colombian "danza", the lyrics have been composed by well-known poets), the guabina (the [tiple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiple), the [bandola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandola) and the [requinto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Requinto) are the basic instruments), the [sanjuanero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanjuanero) (it originated in [Tolima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tolima_Department) and [Huila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huila_Department) Departments, the rhythm is joyful and fast).[^cite_note-453][^cite_note-454][^cite_note-455][^cite_note-456][^cite_note-457] Apart from these traditional rhythms, [salsa music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salsa_music) has spread throughout the country, and the city of [Cali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cali) is considered by many salsa singers to be 'The New Salsa Capital of the World'.[^cite_note-Colombian_music-442][^cite_note-458][^cite_note-459]

The instruments that distinguish the music of the [Eastern Plains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orinoqu%C3%ADa_natural_region) are the [harp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harp), the [cuatro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuatro_\(instrument\)) (a type of four-stringed guitar) and maracas. Important rhythms of this region are the [joropo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joropo) (a fast rhythm and there is also tapping as a result of its [flamenco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flamenco) ancestry) and the [galeron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galeron) (it is heard a lot while [cowboys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cowboy) are working).[^cite_note-Colombian_music-442][^cite_note-460][^cite_note-461][^cite_note-462]

The music of the Amazon region is strongly influenced by the indigenous religious practices. Some of the musical instruments used are the manguaré (a musical instrument of [ceremonial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceremonial) type, consisting of a pair of large [cylindrical drums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cylindrical_drum)), the [quena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quena) (melodic instrument), the [rondador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rondador), the [congas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conga), [bells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bell), and different types of flutes.[^cite_note-463][^cite_note-464][^cite_note-465]

The music of the [Archipelago of San Andrés, Providencia and Santa Catalina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archipelago_of_San_Andr%C3%A9s,_Providencia_and_Santa_Catalina) is usually accompanied by a [mandolin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandolin), a [tub-bass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washtub_bass), a [jawbone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jawbone_\(instrument\)), a guitar and [maracas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maraca). Some popular archipelago rhythms are the [Schottische](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schottische), the [Calypso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calypso_music), the [Polka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polka) and the [Mento](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mento).[^cite_note-466][^cite_note-467]

### Popular culture

Main articles: [Theater of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theater_of_Colombia), [Cinema of Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Colombia), and [Mass media in Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_media_in_Colombia)



The [Cartagena Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cartagena_Film_Festival) is the oldest cinema event in Latin America. The central focus is on films from Ibero-America.

[^cite_note-fiapf-468]

Theater was introduced in Colombia during the [Spanish colonization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_colonization_of_the_Americas) in 1550 through [zarzuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zarzuela) companies. Colombian theater is supported by the Ministry of Culture and a number of private and state owned organizations. The [Ibero-American Theater Festival of Bogotá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theater_of_Colombia) is the cultural event of the highest importance in Colombia and one of the biggest theater festivals in the world.[^cite_note-Theater_Festival-469] Other important theater events are: The Festival of Puppet The Fanfare (Medellín), The Manizales Theater Festival, The Caribbean Theatre Festival (Santa Marta) and The Art Festival of Popular Culture "Cultural Invasion" (Bogotá).[^cite_note-Theatre_in_Colombia-470][^cite_note-theatrical_production-471][^cite_note-teatro_siglo_XX-472]

Although the [Colombian cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Colombia) is young as an industry, more recently the film industry was growing with support from the Film Act passed in 2003.[^cite_note-the_Film_Act_passed_in_2003-473] Many film festivals take place in Colombia, but the two most important are the [Cartagena Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cartagena_Film_Festival), which is the oldest film festival in Latin America, and the [Bogotá Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bogot%C3%A1_Film_Festival).[^cite_note-film_festivals-474][^cite_note-475]

Some important national circulation newspapers are *[El Tiempo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Tiempo_\(Colombia\))* and *[El Espectador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Espectador)*. [Television in Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_in_Colombia) has two privately owned TV networks and three state-owned TV networks with national coverage, as well as six regional TV networks and dozens of local TV stations. Private channels, [RCN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RCN_TV) and [Caracol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caracol_Televisi%C3%B3n) are the highest-rated. The regional channels and regional newspapers cover a department or more and its content is made in these particular areas.[^cite_note-Television_in_Colombia-476][^cite_note-press-477][^cite_note-The_press_in_Colombia-478]

Colombia has three major national [radio networks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_in_Colombia): [Radiodifusora Nacional de Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radiodifusora_Nacional_de_Colombia), a state-run national radio; [Caracol Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caracol_Radio) and [RCN Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RCN_Radio), privately owned networks with hundreds of affiliates. There are other national networks, including [Cadena Super](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cadena_Super), [Todelar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Todelar), and Colmundo. Many hundreds of radio stations are registered with the [Ministry of Information Technologies and Communications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Information_Technologies_and_Communications_\(Colombia\)).[^cite_note-Radio_in_Colombia-479]

### Cuisine

Main article: [Colombian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_cuisine)



Bandeja paisa (top) and Ajiaco (bottom) are two of the most traditional plates in the country.

Colombia's varied cuisine is influenced by its diverse fauna and flora as well as the cultural traditions of the ethnic groups. Colombian dishes and ingredients vary widely by region. Some of the most common ingredients are: cereals such as rice and maize; tubers such as potato and [cassava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cassava); assorted [legumes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legume); meats, including beef, chicken, pork and goat; fish; and seafood.[^cite_note-Paseo_de_olla-480][^cite_note-481] Colombia cuisine also features a variety of tropical fruits such as [cape gooseberry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physalis_peruviana), [feijoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acca_sellowiana), [arazá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugenia_stipitata), [dragon fruit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dragon_fruit), [mangosteen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangosteen), [granadilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passiflora_ligularis), [papaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papaya), [guava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guava), mora ([blackberry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blackberry)), [lulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solanum_quitoense), [soursop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annona_muricata) and [passionfruit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passionfruit).[^cite_note-Colombian_cuisine-482] Colombia is one of the world's largest consumers of fruit juices.[^cite_note-483]

Among the most representative appetizers and soups are [patacones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tostones) (fried green plantains), [sancocho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sancocho) de gallina (chicken soup with root vegetables) and [ajiaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajiaco) (potato and corn soup). Representative snacks and breads are [pandebono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandebono), [arepas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arepa) (corn cakes), [aborrajados](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aborrajado) (fried sweet plantains with cheese), [torta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torta) de [choclo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choclo), [empanadas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empanada) and [almojábanas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Almoj%C3%A1bana). Representative main courses are [bandeja paisa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandeja_paisa), [lechona tolimense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lechona_tolimense), [mamona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ternera_a_la_llanera), [tamales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamales) and fish dishes (such as [arroz de lisa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arroz_de_lisa)), especially in coastal regions where [kibbeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kibbeh), [suero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suero), [costeño cheese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coste%C3%B1o_cheese) and [carimañolas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carima%C3%B1ola) are also eaten. Representative side dishes are papas chorreadas (potatoes with cheese), remolachas rellenas con huevo duro (beets stuffed with [hard-boiled egg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard-boiled_egg)) and [arroz con coco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coconut_rice) (coconut rice).[^cite_note-Colombian_cuisine-482][^cite_note-Paseo_de_olla-480] [Organic food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organic_food) is a current trend in big cities, although in general across the country the fruits and veggies are very natural and fresh.[^cite_note-484][^cite_note-Colombian_Food-485]

Representative desserts are [buñuelos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bu%C3%B1uelo), [natillas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natilla), [Maria Luisa cake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Luisa_cake), [bocadillo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bocadillo_\(dessert\)) made of guayaba (guava jelly), [cocadas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cocadas) (coconut balls), casquitos de guayaba (candied guava peels), [torta de natas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torta_de_nata), [obleas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oblea), [flan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cr%C3%A8me_caramel) de [mango](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mango), [roscón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosc%C3%B3n), [milhoja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milhoja), [manjar blanco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manjar_blanco), dulce de [feijoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feijoa), dulce de [papayuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_papaya), [torta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torta) de mojicón, and esponjado de [curuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curuba). Typical sauces (salsas) are [hogao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hogao) (tomato and onion sauce) and Colombian-style [ají](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aj%C3%AD_\(sauce\)).[^cite_note-Colombian_cuisine-482][^cite_note-Paseo_de_olla-480]

Some representative beverages are [coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coffee_production_in_Colombia) (Tinto), [champús](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champ%C3%BAs), [cholado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cholado), [lulada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lulada), [avena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avena_\(beverage\)) colombiana, [sugarcane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugarcane) juice, [aguapanela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aguapanela), [aguardiente](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aguardiente), [hot chocolate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_chocolate) and fresh fruit juices (often made with water or milk).[^cite_note-Colombian_cuisine-482][^cite_note-Paseo_de_olla-480]

### Sports

Main article: [Sport in Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Colombia)



[Mariana Pajón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariana_Paj%C3%B3n) is a Colombian cyclist, two-time Olympic gold medalist and [BMX World Champion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UCI_BMX_World_Championships).

[Tejo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tejo_\(sport\)) is Colombia's national sport and is a team sport that involves launching projectiles to hit a target.[^cite_note-Tejo-Colombia's_national_sport.-486] But of all sports in Colombia, [football](10568.md) is the most popular. [Colombia](680175.md) was the champion of the [2001 Copa América](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001_Copa_Am%C3%A9rica), in which they set a new record of being undefeated, conceding no goals and winning each match. Colombia has been awarded "[mover of the year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Rankings#Best_Mover_of_the_Year)" twice.[^cite_note-487]

Colombia is a hub for [roller skaters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inline_speed_skating). The national team is a perennial powerhouse at the World Roller Speed Skating Championships.[^cite_note-Patinaje_colombiano-488] Colombia has traditionally been very good in [cycling](857311.md) and a large number of Colombian cyclists have triumphed in major competitions of cycling.[^cite_note-Colombian_cycling-489]

Baseball is popular in cities like [Cartagena](63927.md) and [Barranquilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barranquilla). Of those cities have come good players like: [Orlando Cabrera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orlando_Cabrera), [Édgar Rentería](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89dgar_Renter%C3%ADa), who was champion of the [World Series](23489161.md) in [1997](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_World_Series) and [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_World_Series)[^cite_note-490] and others who have played in [Major League Baseball](38776.md). Colombia was [world amateur champion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baseball_World_Cup) in 1947 and 1965.[^cite_note-béisbolglorias-491]

[Boxing](4243.md) is one of the sports that has produced more world champions for Colombia.[^cite_note-History_of_boxing-492][^cite_note-Boxing_champions-493] [Motorsports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorsport) also occupies an important place in the sporting preferences of Colombians; [Juan Pablo Montoya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Pablo_Montoya) is a race car driver known for winning 7 Formula One events. Colombia also has excelled in sports such as [BMX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UCI_BMX_World_Championships), [judo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judo), [shooting sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shooting_sports), [taekwondo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taekwondo), [wrestling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wrestling), [high diving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_diving) and [athletics](43719.md), also has a long tradition in [weightlifting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_weightlifting) and [bowling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bowling).[^cite_note-494][^cite_note-495][^cite_note-496]
